- **Typography, nine-slice, control states, focus overrides, and transitions.** Text/Button/List typography adds font face, bold/italic, outline color/width, and letter spacing. Nine-slice is normalized off except for Picture/System images. Sparse focused, pressed, and disabled overrides inherit base appearance; directional Up/Down/Left/Right targets win when valid and otherwise fall back to geometric navigation. Interfaces support `none`, `fade`, and `slideLeft` open/close transitions with input locked until opening completes and one-shot close completion; overlay visibility supports fade only.
- **Capture is documented as a visual draft.** The draw log can generate recognized Box/Text/Image/Button/Gauge nodes or import a window bitmap as a Picture, but it cannot infer arbitrary plugin behavior, direct canvas operations, complete transactions, touch controls, or every subclass override. Shop and Battle capture remain visual references, not replacement adapters. Capture itself only updates the editor cache/reference; node imports remain unsaved database edits, while the explicit Picture fallback immediately copies its PNG into `img/pictures`.
- **Real Web and NW.js save smokes.** `tests/smoke` contains a dependency-free W3C WebDriver client. The Web smoke builds and serves the real browser editor, gates `RPGReactorHost.flush`, proves `ProjectController.saveProject()` awaits it, verifies the IndexedDB record, reloads, and checks restoration. The NW smoke launches the actual editor through its matching SDK ChromeDriver, loads a temporary mapless project, saves through `ProjectController`, and verifies the native file. They run outside `node --test` through `npm run smoke:web` / `smoke:nw`; CI downloads and hash-verifies the pinned 0.107.0 SDK before running both under Xvfb.
- **Validation.** The complete suite discovers **1,954 Node tests**, adding Resource Manager model validation/staging/reservation/rollback, modern image imports and runtime consumers, Video Surface serialization/scanning/backends/waits/lifecycle/live-authoring contracts, actor-preview disposal, codec hashes/provenance/legal packaging, and expanded localization coverage. All 1,954 pass with the new files tracked. Source syntax, `npm audit` (zero vulnerabilities), runtime-focused suites, and `git diff --check` pass. Existing real Chromium/NW.js persistence, NW.js layout, and read-only State/Animation screenshot results remain valid. Manual GIF playback, live 2D/3D Video Surface authoring/navigation, actor-preview performance, and final toolbar-icon checks remain open.
- **Historical interface milestones below.** The following capture-to-node and three-baseline entries record earlier stages from this cycle. Automatic capture import was superseded by the explicit Starting Layout/Add to Front actions above, and the original three generated records were extended to seven without renumbering them.
- **Capture → nodes, Gauge node.** Runtime: `ReactorUI.installCaptureHooks()` (from `beginCapture`, after plugins) wraps `Bitmap.prototype.drawText/blt/fillRect/gradientFillRect/clear/clearRect` into a per-bitmap `__rrDraws` log and, with the primitives suppressed underneath, the semantic draws: `Window_Base.drawTextEx` / `drawItemName` (text with codes), `drawCurrencyValue` (`\GOLD` + `\C[16]\G`), `Window_StatusBase.drawActorName/Class/Nickname/Level/Face/Character` (`\P[n]`, `\PCLASS[n]`, `\PLV[n]`, `partyFace` by slot), `Window_Command.drawItem` (a `button` element with the command symbol and enabled flag). `performCapture` writes `windows[i].elements` (window pixels) from `windowElements` — contents log through `elementsFromDraws` (text primitives merged per line into one run with `\C[n]`/`\I[n]` by `mergeTextRuns`; a lone run wider than its max width keeps the width and gets `fitText`; y re-expressed for a text node's line box), inner children and plugin-added child sprites through `spriteElements` (`Sprite_Gauge` → gauge element, `Sprite_Name` → text, image sprites → image by URL folder, drawn sprites → their log) — and `elements` for the scene's own sprites (title art, the drawn game title; window layer, spritesets, the background snapshot and `Sprite_Button` skipped). `imageSourceFromUrl` maps `img/<folder>/` to a node source; sub-frames of system sheets are dropped. Editor: `nodesFromCapture(data, {which, includeScene, nextId})` / `nodeFromElement` (pure, tested) build a Box per visible window (opacity 0 → `fill: none`, never a faded box) with children; `actionForSymbol` wires stock symbols; `addAllFromCapture` runs automatically when a capture lands in a record with no nodes, the list header has Add All, rows have Add (and Picture for windows with a contents PNG: copied to `img/pictures/Capture_<class>[_n].png`, Image node at the client area). Gauge node (`gauge` hp/mp/tp/variable, `index` party slot, `variableId`, `max`, `label`, `showLabel`, `showValue`; 128×24 default): runtime `Window_ReactorUINode.drawGauge` adds a `Sprite_ReactorUIGauge` (lazy subclass of `Sprite_Gauge`, sized to the node, bar = lower half, fonts scaled by height/24, variable kind reads `$gameVariables`) as an inner child with its opacity mirrored from `contentsOpacity`; the editor canvas draws it from the skin colours (19 back, 20/21 HP, 22/23 MP, 28/29 TP). Image sources gain `title1`/`title2`. `Window_ReactorUINode.convertEscapeCharacters` now runs `convertPartyCodes` first, so `\PCLASS[n]` draws as the class (it drew `[n]`: `currentText` converted, `drawTextEx` did not). Fourteen phrases added by hand to all 17 locales. Live rig `ui-capture-nodes.mjs` (session scratchpad): Demo menu → 48 nodes (8 buttons wired, `\C[6]`, `\PCLASS[n]`), title → 6, both booted as previews.

- **Stock interface baselines.** A project that has never authored an interface (no `data/UserInterfaces.json`) used to open the User Interfaces section on an empty list; it now opens on three records generated from its own settings by `src/utils/StockInterfaces.js` (`RRStockInterfaces.build(data)`, seeded in `DatabaseManager.loadProject`, written with the next save): Title Screen, Main Menu and Game End, laid out with the runtime's own window math (`Scene_Title` / `Scene_Menu` / `Scene_GameEnd` rects against `advanced.uiAreaWidth/Height`, `titleCommandWindow` offsets), buttons named from Terms > Commands and gated by System > Menu Commands, wired to the stock actions (New Game script, Continue → load enabled by `DataManager.isAnySavefileExists()`, Save enabled by `$gameSystem.isSaveEnabled()`, To Title, Cancel). Each record carries `stock: "<kind>"`. The game keeps its stock scenes until a record is called or set as the boot interface, so seeding changes nothing at play time. For the Main Menu's party area the runtime gains an image source `partyFace` (the face of the party member in slot `index`) and party codes resolved before the stock escape codes: `\GOLD`, `\PLV[n]`, `\PCLASS[n]`, `\PHP[n]`, `\PMHP[n]`, `\PMP[n]`, `\PMMP[n]`, `\PTP[n]` (n = party slot, 1-based; an empty slot reads as nothing); the editor previews both from System > Starting Party (`startingMember`, `startingStat`: class params at the initial level) and `\P[n]` now previews the same way. Two phrases in 17 locales. Verified live on Star Shift Rebellion (YEP terms with their icon codes, 1280×720). Tests: `stock-interfaces.test.cjs`.
- **Interface capture.** Runtime (`reactor_ui.js`): `ReactorUI.captureRequest()` reads `rrcapture=<scene>&rrcapturedir=<encoded dir>` off the launch line (`launchTokens()` unifies `location.search` and `nw.App.argv`, split on `&`); `Scene_Boot.start` hands a request to `beginCapture`: title goes straight to `Scene_Title`; everything else sets up a new game and starts `Scene_Map`, and once the map has run 30 frames `openCaptureScene` snaps the background and pushes the scene (battle sets up the first troop; shop gets the first six items as goods), which is the flow plugins assume (Star Shift Rebellion's `Irina_PerformanceUpgrade` dereferenced the missing map snapshot when the menu was booted directly). The per-frame `updateCapture` is hooked onto `SceneManager.updateMain` at boot, after every plugin and the MV layer have replaced what they replace (a load-time `updateScene` wrapper vanished under MV projects); it accepts MZ's `scene.isStarted()` or MV's `_sceneStarted`, forces `isGameActive` so an unfocused capture window still updates, waits until every window is open (or 180 frames; or captures as-is after 600 frames of nothing starting), wraps `SceneManager.catchException` so a plugin crash inside the scene is written as the capture's error and the game exits instead of the editor waiting two minutes, reads the screenshot by the screen frame rather than the stage's bounds (an off-screen 3D pass sprite made the battle capture's texture too big to allocate), then `performCapture` writes `capture.json` (scene, size, active plugins, windows: class, rect via tree-accumulated x/y, padding, opacities, openness, cursor rect, windowskin) plus `screen.png` (`renderer.extract.canvas` of the stage) and `window-N.png` per contents bitmap, and `SceneManager.exit()`. Editor: `PlaytestManager.captureScene` / `captureMode`; new `src/utils/EditorCache.js` (`RREditorCache.dir(leaf, projectPath, ...)`, same platform rule as the profiles); `DatabaseUserInterfaceEditor` gains the scene select + Capture button with status, `capture()` (saves, clears the folder, launches, polls for `capture.json` up to 120 s), `loadCapture` / `loadLastCapture` (localStorage per project key), the reference layer in `render()` (drawn under the nodes, opacity slider), `renderCaptureList` with a selected window outlined on the canvas, and `addBoxFromCapture`. `DatabaseEditorUI._selectEntryMarkup(type)` renders a first-run panel for an empty User Interfaces section ("No interfaces yet", what the section is for, Create Interface → the list's New, then the new record opened). Thirteen phrases in 17 locales. Verified live: the Demo's PSYCHRONIC menu (43 plugins, 6 windows) and Star Shift Rebellion's YEP menu (167 plugins, 4 windows) captured over their maps, through the launch line and the tab. Tests: `user-interface-capture.test.cjs`.
- **Custom user interfaces, phase 1.** `runtime/reactor_ui.js` (new, loads after `reactor_windows.js` and before `reactor_mv_compat.js` so the MV window wrapper sees its classes) reads the optional `data/UserInterfaces.json` through the same disk-probe path as the 3D sidecar — never through `DataManager._databaseFiles`, whose watchdog would retry a missing file forever. `Scene_ReactorUI` extends `Scene_MenuBase`; every node is a `Window_ReactorUINode` (a `Window_Base` that hides its frame and back unless the node uses the skin), laid out with anchored offsets against its parent's rectangle, so text uses `drawTextEx` and every escape code, and windows-restyling plugins apply. Focus moves by nearest-in-direction with wraparound, touch hover focuses and click activates, `\V[n]` text redraws when its value changes, and a resume stack restores the interface id when a pushed stock scene pops back (SceneManager rebuilds the scene from its class alone). `PluginManager.registerCommand("RPGReactor", "CallUserInterface")` backs the new Reactor-tab command; `test&rrui=N` on the launch line (the editor's Playtest Interface button) is a preview: `Scene_Boot.start` runs `DataManager.setupNewGame()` for the game objects escape codes and actions read, then `goto(Scene_ReactorUI)` with no title and no map; `ReactorUI._preview` paints a black `ScreenSprite` behind the interface whatever its background setting, and closing the root interface (close, closeAll, a common event, the title action) calls `ReactorUI.endPreview()` → `SceneManager.exit()` — the same exit battle test uses — so the preview never falls through into a playable game. Sub-interfaces and pushed stock scenes still pop back to the preview. Text and Button nodes gain `fitText`: `Window_ReactorUINode.applyFit` bisects a font scale (8 steps, floor `ReactorUI.MIN_FONT_SIZE` = 8 px) until `textSizeEx` of the (re-wrapped) label fits every authored dimension, and `resetFontSettings` applies the scale; the editor's `layoutText` runs the same search over `parseText(node, scale)` so the canvas matches. Editor: `DatabaseUserInterfaceEditor` (canvas at `uiAreaWidth × uiAreaHeight` with a Canvas 2D port of the window-skin drawing, node tree, property panel, undo/redo, drag/resize with 8 handles and snap), `CallUserInterfaceEditor`, `DatabaseManager` support that reads an absent file as `[null]` and writes no file for a project with no interfaces, and 62 new phrases hand-authored into all 17 locales. Verified live in NW.js on the Demo's sample interface: boot into it, keyboard path, hit-testing, push/pop of Scene_Save, close to the map, reopen through the 357 path, and the editor's drag, resize, add, undo, and dialogs, with no console errors. A refinement pass then swept the editor at 1280–2560 px, two light palettes, an empty record, and three authored kinds (title screen, yes/no dialog, HUD panel): the property panel became one label|field|label|field grid that collapses pairs when narrow, the workspace stacks below 1300 px of detail width and fits the viewport with panels scrolling inside, the canvas preview loads the project's main font through `FontFace` and uses MZ's line metrics, changing a node's parent or anchor keeps it in place on screen, condition scripts compile once, and cancel closes a button-less interface whose cancel action is Nothing. A third pass built a character sheet (nested columns, a wrapped bio, a label over a portrait, a 3×3 icon grid, a half-opacity group) and added what it needed: word wrap on Text nodes, Image nodes as parents, opacity that cascades to children, parents-first draw order kept on load and through ▲/▼ (which now move sibling subtrees), a same-row preference for directional wrap-around, and a ◐ marker in the tree for conditionally visible nodes.
- **The FPS counter names its renderer.** The F2 counter reads `144 FPS` on a shared baseline with "WebGL", "WebGPU", or "Canvas" centred on its own row beneath, as RPG Maker's meter did. The counter is a flex column that sizes to its content (min-width 90px) instead of absolute offsets in a fixed 90×40 box, so a three-digit frame rate no longer runs into the label. Every inline style now resets the properties MZ's stock `css/game.css` sets on `#fpsCounterBox/Label/Number` (absolute positioning, paddings, 30px heights, a 32px line-height, opacity 0.8) — projects created from MZ carry that sheet, and an inline declaration only beats an id selector for properties it actually sets, which is what had been stacking the text on itself. MV's `Graphics.showFps` shim shows the box as `flex`. The PIXI app initializes asynchronously, so the mode is read on the counter's own update tick rather than at creation; `Graphics.rendererModeName(renderer)` resolves it polymorphically (v8 `name`/`RendererType`, v5–v7 `RENDERER_TYPE` — the numeric values collide, so the string wins and the enum is taken from the loaded PIXI). Covered by `fps-counter-renderer-mode.test.cjs`.

### Fixed

- **Plugin Manager external links and scrollbars.** Parsed plugin URLs now carry the editor's `external-link` marker and `noreferrer`, so `UIManager` cancels NW.js navigation and dispatches the URL through `nw.Shell.openExternal()` to the user's default browser. Every Plugin Manager scroll surface, including the load-order list, details pane, help viewer, Add Plugin list, and nested structure/array editors, reuses `rr-accent-scrollbar`; the shared class now also declares standards-based theme colors and thin width alongside its Chromium gradient thumb.
- **Animation list and asset-picker polish.** The Animation database bypasses the generic 250-row append batches and renders its complete filtered index, so the native scrollbar reflects all 5,000 possible rows without thumb rebasing. Preview battleback triggers delegate to `showImagePicker` with recursive folders, thumbnails, search, alphabet rail, current selection, and `(None)`. Select Effect File keeps its transformed live Effekseer canvas in a wider right pane while replacing the flat list with `RRPickerIndex`, accent scrollbars, folders, Unicode search, keyboard navigation, explicit None, focus containment/restoration, mutation tracking, request-generation race guards, and complete pending/loaded effect cleanup. Pick SE delegates to `RRAudioPickerModal` with cover art, seek, transport, nested folders, and volume/pitch/pan; timing forms author and preserve pan in both MZ and sprite data. Duration replaces Chromium's native spinner with themed up/down controls. The shared image picker's Open in Folder action uses the secondary-button theme.
- **Futuristic tool icon set.** Undo/Redo, pencil/single, rectangle, circle, Fill, Shadow Pen, Eraser, Auto/Layer 1-4, Database, Plugins, Resource Manager, Audio, and Forge use dedicated high-contrast deep-blue/cyan SVGs with gold and magenta accents. Fill is a tilted bucket pouring paint; Shadow Pen has a cool-metal fountain nib visibly darkening the tile instead of sparking it; Undo/Redo are mirrored curved arrows. Web layouts retain horizontal toolbar scrolling; base desktop overflow behavior is unchanged.
- **Actor/model preview lifecycle.** `ModelGraphicPicker` generation-cancels superseded loads, disposes replaced templates, and avoids the HiDPI resize feedback loop. `Database3DBindings` deduplicates concurrent cold thumbnail renders and performs one close resynchronization. `ModelPreview3D` owns deterministic geometry/material/texture/object-URL/control/renderer/context cleanup. Rapid selection and project changes no longer leave stale blank previews, duplicate expensive renders, or leaked WebGL contexts.
- **GitHub #24, Effekseer preview and runtime parity.** `AnimationPickerModal` accepts either a project manager or direct project path, safely handles synchronous cached-load callbacks, coalesces repeated pending selections, uses an opaque canvas for correct additive output, and shares a persisted light/mid/dark backdrop with the event animation picker. Plugin `animation`/`animation[]` references and Show Battle Animation now open that live picker while preserving their string/positive-ID contracts and generic fallback. The Animations page blits its Canvas2D battleback, target, and flashes into WebGL before `captureBackground()`, uploads only when that scene changes, restores Effekseer's GL state, and applies the selected animation's offset, rotation, scale, and speed in the effect-file preview. Every editor, Forge, diagnostic, and runtime surface uses aspect-correct positive-Y projection and authored X rotation, avoiding the old depth-reversing 180-degree compensation. Runtime revision `20260828.1` refreshes existing same-version projects once while preserving plugins; `reactor_main.js` is copied last so a failed refresh cannot falsely mark a partial runtime current.
- **GitHub #25, Add State Normal Attack sentinel.** `DatabaseEffectEditor` prepends `dataId: 0` as Normal Attack for code 21 only, keeps it selected through render and interaction round trips, and leaves Remove State restricted to real state IDs. Summary text now calls zero Normal Attack only for Add State; a malformed Remove State/0 row reports honestly as State #0.
- **GitHub #26, complete image browse controls.** `DatabaseEditorUI.imageBrowser`, `browseImageFolder`, and `createImageBrowseButton` route bare image-name fields through the existing recursive, searchable preview picker without removing direct text entry. Show Picture, Change Battle Background, Change Parallax, Change Actor Images, and Change Vehicle Image cover eight fields; character and face sheets synchronize their chosen cell indices through the fields' existing input events. Map Properties and the Troop editor add Browse controls beside both retained battleback dropdowns, support clearing through the pinned `(None)` action, and accept files discovered after the dropdown was populated. Event-command calls lend the shared modal z-index 10007 and the next ordinary caller gets its base level back. Map parallax selection now passes the real file list with `allowNone` instead of inserting a translated sentinel and transparent preview asset. Five direct phrases are reviewed in all 17 non-English locales.
- **Local-reviewed localization architecture and routing audit.** `I18nReviewedTranslations.js` is a checked-in final-precedence layer over the legacy broad catalog and curated manager tables, with reviewed `text`, `keyed`, `commands`, and `sections` maps for every non-English locale. Corrections cover RPG-specific Party/Actor/MP terminology, build-worker wording, copied Greek/Turkish text, Thai orthography, and all 122 Polish event commands plus 22 sections. Newly localized paths include project/package diagnostics with placeholder-safe interpolation, map grid/anchor/range/parallax chrome, 3D rig status, unnamed-map and option-value fallbacks, picker search, project lock/playtest errors, Quick Events, and Tileset flags/3D tools. Animation timing uses a localized `None` label only for display rather than persisting it as an SE filename. The unused Microsoft-edge generator was deleted, leaving no online translation path. `i18n.test.cjs` now pins reviewed precedence, exact locale coverage, placeholders across all four catalogs, known Greek leakage, normalized Thai, complete Polish tables, and static source coverage; focused routing tests protect the changed UI.
- **Audio event-command player parity.** `AudioCommandEditor` now sends Play BGM/BGS/ME/SE directly to `RRAudioPickerModal` instead of rendering its older duplicated inline player. The shared current UI supplies cover art, recursive folders, Unicode ordering, thumbnails, seek/transport/loop controls, `(None)`, and live volume/pitch/pan while returning exactly `{name, volume, pitch, pan}` as the command's sole parameter. Troop insert/edit paths join map and Common Event paths through the same editor, with complete defaults for Play ME, fades, and Stop SE; plugin audio arguments loop BGM/BGS/ME previews like the base player. `AudioPickerModal` now reads native loop points, wraps per frame with modulo, preserves tail audition after a seek, honors the main player's Wine `htmlAudioOnly` policy, and closes a partially initialized Web Audio context before falling back.
- **State, trait-table, and database chrome polish.** `DatabaseStateEditor` now lays out Duration and Messages as controls-first grids, keeps dependent turn/damage/walking rows aligned, and documents `%1` as the Actor/Enemy Name token. Actor/Class/Weapon/Armor/State/Enemy trait headers use a two-cell row with Type spanning the hidden selection indicator instead of rendering an empty spur. The Database list scrollbar is palette-aware, Change Maximum suppresses Chromium's native number spinner while retaining keyboard stepping, selected-row contrast uses defined theme tokens, and recent System/audio/plugin modals preserve accessible names, focus containment/restoration, Escape/backdrop behavior, and narrow-width reflow.
- **GitHub #13, animation-preview fidelity.** MV sprite previews run their update loop at 60 Hz while advancing authored cells every four ticks, so target, screen, and hide flashes fire and decay at the same rate as the game. MZ/Effekseer previews consume target flash timings and keep updating through late timing/flash completion after the effect itself ends. Sprite cells map all four blend modes to Canvas composition, and head/center/feet/screen anchors now agree across rendering, hit testing, and dropped-cell coordinates. Frame-zero timings seed static views immediately; frame/timing edits redraw those views; sprite strips restore nearest-neighbor sampling after resize; playback resets its counters and flash state; Effekseer catch-up consumes every tick, balances `beginDraw`/`endDraw` with `try/finally`, and generation-guards delayed readiness retries. SE volume preserves an authored zero. Effekseer background capture/compositing and asynchronous browser effect loading remain separate follow-ups.
- **GitHub #18/#22, complete plugin schema and command-block handling.** `PluginAnnotations.js`, `PluginParamWidgets.js`, `PluginParamCodec.js`, and `PluginDataRefs.js` now provide one path for top-level parameters, nested structs, and plugin command arguments. This covers choices, all 19 database/System reference types and arrays, color values, authored Boolean labels, multiline values, recursive structs, malformed raw text, exact RPG Maker string/JSON encoding, and `img/...` file fields with recursive folders plus image/dimension previews. Web command dialogs receive the host filesystem, and encrypted Web previews decrypt asynchronously, including key recovery. `PluginCommandEditor` emits ordered readable 657 rows while retaining unknown rows under partial metadata; `EventCommandList`, Common Events, and Troops treat 357/657 and 355/655 as indivisible blocks for insertion, replacement, selection, clipboard operations, and direct continuation editing. Complex dialogs stack above command modals and Cancel never mutates the outer draft.
- **GitHub #21, escaped desktop asset URLs.** `EncryptedAssets.fileUrl` now uses `pathToFileURL` with explicit Windows handling only for drive and UNC paths, leaving native relative/root semantics native. Its fallback produces equivalent encoded URLs. Character, face, battler, tileset, database-icon, event-face, and IconSet consumers now use the shared resolver. Spaces, reserved URL characters, Unicode, POSIX double-slash paths, Windows drives, and UNC shares have regression coverage; encrypted `data:` URLs and WebHost's resolver are unchanged.
- **GitHub #20, recursive asset picker folders.** `RRPickerIndex.buildFolderTree` and `foldersLeadingTo` preserve complete relative values while producing arbitrary-depth, descendant-counted folder nodes. `createBrowser` filters before tree construction, opens all selected ancestors, renders folders before files, and gives folder headers focus, ARIA state, Enter/Space toggles, and Left/Right controls. `AudioPickerModal` uses the same tree without narrowing its playback lookup, keeps flat A-Z behavior, and exposes equivalent keyboard controls. Character, tileset, database image, title, model, and message-face callers enable folder mode.
- **GitHub #19, scaled window filter rectangles.** `Window._updateFilterArea` writes dimensions in world scale, and the PIXI 8 localization step divides both position and dimensions back into client-local coordinates using matrix-column magnitudes. Half-scale/plugin/stock-style rectangles, nonuniform reflected or rotated transforms, zero scale, stale transforms, and pre-v8 behavior are covered. All tracked runtime copies are synchronized.
- **Save success now means durable browser storage, and Reactor sidecars are atomic.** `ProjectController.saveAll` retains the old project/map/database dirty baselines until `RPGReactorHost.flush()` resolves; a rejected IndexedDB transaction restores those baselines, reports the storage error, and returns false. Desktop writes for `Database.r3d.json`, map/tileset 3D sidecars, `model.json`, and `model.rig.bin` route through `RRWriteFileAtomicSync`. Sidecar reads distinguish a missing file from malformed or unreadable content, so an edit cannot silently replace damaged authored data.

- **User Interfaces tab: fit, keys, capture scope (owner pass).** Layout: `.rr-ui-workspace` is `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 380px` with `grid-auto-rows: minmax(0, 1fr)` (the `clamp(420px, 100vh - 360px, 960px)` estimate overshot 1080p by 108 px); `fitCanvas` scales to the host's height as well as width when the workspace's `--rr-ui-fit` custom property reads `both` (the ≤1300 px stacked layout sets `width`); the Note textarea is a full-width row of General Settings; side columns are `minmax(170px, 200px)` / `minmax(320px, 360px)`. Keys: one `onKey` on the editor wrapper handles Delete/Backspace, Ctrl+D/Z/Y (arrows stay canvas-only) and `stopPropagation`s; tree rows and captured-window rows are `tabIndex -1` and take focus on mousedown, `renderTree`/`renderCaptureList` restore focus after rebuilding rows (a re-render dropped focus to the body, where the record list's document-level Delete cleared the record); `DatabaseEditorUI`'s list key handler ignores events whose target is inside `#database-detail`. Capture: the reference layer is per open view — `loadLastCapture` and the `rrui.lastCapture.*` localStorage key are gone, `clearReference()` + a Clear chip, the scene dropdown loads a cached capture on demand; `capture()` no longer reaches for a `saveProject` (it was undefined on the parent editor anyway) and appends "Unsaved changes are not in the capture." to the status when `projectController.hasUnsavedChanges()`; `addNode` returns the node, so `addBoxFromCapture` actually applies the window rect, `opacity` and `backOpacity → fillOpacity` (it returned early before, leaving a default box, and pushed a second undo step); `showUserInterfaceDetail` calls `detach()` first so window mousemove/mouseup listeners stop accumulating. Two phrases added to all 17 locales by hand.
- **GPU-side load.** `Window.clipWithMask` (default true, PIXI 8 only): `_createClientArea` keeps `filters = []` and a live `filterArea` for readers but clips through a `PIXI.Graphics` stencil rect child of `_clientArea`, rebuilt by `_updateClipMask` only when origin/inner size change; PIXI's `StencilMask` manages the mask's `includeInBuild`/`measurable` flags, and setting `renderable = false` by hand collects no geometry and clips everything out (found the hard way). Verified on Project3's item screen: pixel-equivalent to the AlphaFilter path, PIXI GPU time 2.12 → 1.96 ms/frame there (the filter passes are the bandwidth part, which weighs more on integrated GPUs). `Reactor3D.sheetTextureFor(bitmap)` (WeakMap) + `billboardView(holder, sheet)`: billboards share a sheet upload and set `repeat`/`offset` per frame (negative repeat mirrors); the canvas copy per frame change is gone. `MapScene.freezeStaticMeshes` after `build`. `Reactor3D.maxTextureSize` (2048) applied by `capBitmapSize` in the worker decode and `capImage` on the `TextureLoader`/`Image` paths (data-URI images bypass the worker; the Demo's Buick and Fleagus were 4096²). GPU timer-query harness `gpu-probe.mjs` (session scratchpad): on this RTX the 3D passes are ~0.5 ms and PIXI ~0.9 ms per frame; MSAA/scale are noise here, so their value is the weak-GPU ratio. Tests: `runtime-gpu-load.test.cjs`; the billboard flipY test now pins the sheet design.
- **Per-frame allocation churn (owner: "the destroy-and-recreate pattern").** `alloc-profile.mjs` (session scratchpad; CDP `HeapProfiler.startSampling`, aggregated by call frame and parent) ranked the sites. Fixes: `forceSinglePass: true` on the four flat double-sided transparent `MeshBasicMaterial`s (three's `renderObject` renders such materials twice and sets `needsUpdate` each pass → `getProgram` → `getParameters` allocations, 32 MB/8.5 s; the additive lights material deliberately keeps two passes, since single-pass halved every light and the frame diffed 3.6%); `modelSpecFromNote` memoised by note string, `normalizeModelSpec` by sidecar object (WeakMap), `hasEventModels` by map data (WeakMap); `projectToScreen` / `standScaleAt` scratch vectors; `applyModelAnimation` caches lowercased part and rule names; `Point`/`Rectangle.prototype.initialize` write fields directly on PIXI 8 (v5-7 keep `PIXISuper`); `Window._clampedCursorRect` returns a per-window scratch rectangle and `_updateFilterArea` applies through two per-window scratch points; `Tilemap.updateTransform` skips the repaint while both layers are hidden (a 3D map in their place) and repaints when they show. Second pass: `applyModelAnimation` draws every Vector3/Quaternion/Matrix4/Euler, action record, and per-mesh match record from `Reactor3D._animPool` (counters reset per call; nothing handed out outlives the call; `entry.accMatrix` persists per mesh) and filters clip rules once per rules array; `projectToScreen(camera, x, y, z, out)` fills a caller-owned record (`standScaleAt` and `Sprite_Character` pass theirs); `Sprite._refresh` on v8 moves the frame of a texture it owns and calls `texture.update()` (the texture is created `dynamic: true` so PIXI's Sprite listens and refreshes bounds; a fresh `PIXI.Texture` is only built on a source change), verified on Project3: same texture uid across frames, `getLocalBounds` follows the frame both ways; `PSYCHRONIC_EventCustomizerMZ.js` caches `$gameMap.isEventRunning()` per frame (it was `events().filter()` twice per event per frame). Walking 250 → 146 MB per 8.5 s, battle 151 → 110 MB; map frame pixel-identical. Tests: `runtime-frame-churn.test.cjs`.
- **Adaptive 3D resolution, overlay redraw discipline, idle preview throttles.** `reactor_3d.js`: `Reactor3D.renderScale` / `adaptiveResolution` / `minRenderScale`; `Viewport.setRenderScale` resizes the targets (`targetSize`) and bumps the generation so the pass sprites rebuild; `samplesForScale` (4 / 2 / 0 at 1 / 0.75 / 0.5); `_trackFrame` keeps an EMA of frame intervals per game frame and feeds `adaptScale` (pure: drop a quarter when the EMA exceeds 1.35× the 60 fps frame, climb when under 1.05× after 300 calm frames, 120-frame cooldown; an earlier draft measured the display period from the fastest interval seen and mistook a catch-up burst for 240 Hz). Battler targets follow the scale (`targetPixels`). `warmLoadedTemplates` no longer allocates a `THREE.Scene` per frame when nothing is pending. `PSYCHRONIC_RaveLighting.js`: `overlayScale` 0.5 (bitmap scaled, drawing under `setTransform`), `toneOverlaySignature` skips redraws when tone, light set, and light sprite positions are unchanged, `animatedRedrawInterval` 2 for flicker/fire/pulsate/flashlight/beam/phase. Editor: `Database3DEditor.shouldRenderPreview` and `MapEditor3D.shouldRender` (idle = 100 ms cadence); activity = easing, drag, load, edit state, ambient rules, recent input/pointer, camera or hover change, flight. Measured: Sword_Fleagus preview 29 renders per 3 s idle, full rate under drag. Tests in `reactor-3d-shared-context.test.cjs` and `db3d-preview-perf.test.cjs`.
- **Shared-context 3D map rendering.** `Reactor3D.Viewport` now initialises in shared mode when `Reactor3D.sharedContextAvailable()` (PIXI 8 with a WebGL2 context, `resetState`, and `THREE.WebGLRenderTarget`; `?r3dcopy` or `Reactor3D.useSharedContext = false` opt out; failures fall back to the canvas path via `_teardownShared`). three's `WebGLRenderer` is built on `Graphics._app.renderer.gl`; each pass (`below` / `above` / `lights`) renders into a `WebGLRenderTarget` (MSAA `renderTargetSamples`, `isXRRenderTarget = true` for the sRGB shader encode and canvas-style tone mapping, `texture.internalFormat = "RGBA8"` so the texture and the multisample renderbuffer match for the resolve blit). `passTexture(slot)` wraps the target through `Reactor3D.adoptGlTexture` (seeds `source._gpuData[renderer.uid]`, disarms `update`, no upload method) into a `PIXI.Texture` rotated `MIRROR_VERTICAL`; `render(slot)` brackets three with `resetState` on both sides and nulls PIXI's bound-texture cache. `Spriteset_Map.createReactor3DSprite` builds shared passes off `passTexture`, `updateReactor3DTexture` skips them, `destroyReactor3DSprite` never destroys a shared texture, and a viewport `generation` bump (resize) rebuilds the sprites. A/B harness `shared-ab.mjs` (session scratchpad): pixel-identical to the copy path, no GL console errors. Battlers: `Reactor3D.paintBattlerFrame(state, sprite)` replaces the two identical per-frame copy blocks in `updateEnemyModelSprite` / `updateActorModelSprite`; on a shared context `_paintBattlerShared` creates a `size×size` target per battler state, adopts it into the Bitmap's `baseTexture.source` (freeing the PIXI-made texture it replaces), re-asserts `MIRROR_VERTICAL` on the sprite texture each frame (MZ's `_refresh` rebuilds the texture on frame change), and `renderInto`s it; `releaseBattlerState` disposes the target on actor/enemy id change and from a `Sprite_Battler.prototype.destroy` wrapper at the end of `reactor_sprites.js`. Viewport gains `createTarget`, `targetHandle`, `renderInto`, `pixi`. Battle A/B: battlers upright, no `_battlerRenderer` context created. Tests: `reactor-3d-shared-context.test.cjs`; three pinning tests updated for the slot argument and `_initializeCanvas`.
- **Runtime 3D instance stalls (owner-reported, follow-on to the preview work).** `reactor_3d.js`: `cloneModelTemplate` detaches `userData.glbTextures` around `template.clone(true)` and reattaches it by reference (three's `Object3D.copy` JSON-copies `userData`; `Texture.toJSON` serialises the image), then calls the new `presetSkinnedBounds(object)`, which sets `frustumCulled = false` and `mesh.boundingSphere` from the shared geometry sphere on every skinned mesh (three r185 `projectObject` calls `SkinnedMesh.computeBoundingSphere`, a per-vertex bone pass, whenever the sphere is null and `sortObjects` is on, culling or not); `applyModelRig` presets the meshes it builds the same way. `pixi_compat.js` `PIXISuper` memoises `__rrEs6Class` per class so only the first call pays the throw-and-regex discovery. Templates synced. Harnesses `game-perf.mjs` (rAF gaps + longtasks + slow-call stacks by window: load / walk / battle) and `game-profile.mjs` (CDP `Profiler` sampling with self/total/caller-chain aggregation) in the session scratchpad. Tests: `reactor-3d-carved-parts.test.cjs` (clone never serialises textures; skinned instances carry preset bounds), `pixi-super-memo.test.cjs`. `Database3DEditor` shows `Loading model…` (17 keyed locales in `I18nManager.js`) via `_refreshHint` while `_loadingPreview`.
- **Database 3D preview stutter (owner-reported).** `Database3DEditor`: `_updateHover` is gated by `shouldRaycastHover` (pointer at rest ≥150 ms via `_pointerMovedAt`, no drag, no wheel/orbit input in 250 ms via `_lastInputAt`, and `_triangleCount ≤ HOVER_TRIANGLE_BUDGET` = 150k); the old interval throttle still fired a whole-mesh `Raycaster.intersectObject` on every pointer move. `_fillThumbnails` awaits `_whenPreviewIdle()` (no `_loadingPreview`, no drag, 600 ms since input) before each uncached render, passes `beforeBuild` to `_loadTemplate` so `Reactor3D.readModelAsync` (new optional 5th `options` argument, runtime + templates) holds the main-thread template build after the worker parse until idle, skips the +1.6 s re-render when `texturesDecoded(glbTextures)`, and reads/writes a per-machine PNG cache (`thumbnailCacheRoot`: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\RPGReactor\ModelThumbnails`, `~/Library/Application Support/RPGReactor/ModelThumbnails`, `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/rpg-reactor/model-thumbnails`; file name = sha1 of source path, size, mtime). `_drawPreview` wraps `_drawPreviewNow` to hold `_loadingPreview`. Harness `db3d-perf.mjs` (session scratchpad): rAF-gap + longtask probe with per-method attribution. Tests: `db3d-preview-perf.test.cjs`.
- **Plugin Manager icon and audio pickers (GitHub report).** New `src/utils/IconPicker.js` (`window.RRIconPicker`: `show`, `createField`, `imageUrl`, `iconSetPathFor`) is the database `showIconPicker` body moved out, with the asset-URL resolution from `applyListIcon`, an `onerror` path (closes, reports through `uiManager.showAlert`), a per-path sheet cache for previews (the picker reloads), and click clamping to the sheet. `DatabaseEditorUI.showIconPicker` delegates. `PluginManager` gains `createIconInput`, `createFileInput` (audio dirs: name + Browse... → `RRAudioPickerModal` with `levels: null`; other dirs: the file dropdown, keeping a saved name the folder no longer holds), `openAudioPicker`, `isAudioDir`, `audioFolderLabel`; branches in `createParameterInputWithDepth` (icon, file via the helper), `createStructFieldInput` (icon, file with `dir`), `showArrayElementEditor` (`icon[]`, `file[]` with `dir`), and `renderArrayStructureEditor`'s `+ Add Element` (audio `file[]` opens the picker; `(None)` adds nothing). `PluginCommandEditor` adds `case 'icon'` and routes audio `@dir` file args through `showAudioPicker` before the generic picker. Tests: `plugin-manager-pickers.test.cjs` (parser carries the types, dir spellings, source routing, the field and picker over a fake DOM, the missing-sheet path); verified live on a fixture plugin in NW.js.
- **New Project dialog, anvil Forge icon, list context menus (GitHub feedback).** `UIManager.showNewProjectDialog({ defaultName, hasTemplate, validate })` resolves `{ name, blank }` or `null`, in `rr-modal` chrome with a radio group for the Reactor One demo (disabled and deselected when no template folder is found) or a blank project; `validate(name)` returns an inline error and keeps the dialog open. `ProjectController.newProject` uses it in place of `prompt()`/`alert()` and passes `{ blank }` to `ProjectManager.createNewProject(targetPath, name, options)`, which then skips the template copy and writes the existing starter scaffold. `FORGE_ICONS.forge` and the toolbar button share a new anvil path. `setupMapTabs` binds `contextmenu` on `#maps-list` and `#quick-access-list` for targets outside a `[data-map-id]` row, routed through a new `showContextMenuItems(x, y, items)` that `showMapContextMenu` now delegates to (`showMapListContextMenu`: New Map at root + Paste Map; `showQuickAccessContextMenu`: toggle the open map). Eleven new phrases hand-authored into all 17 locales. Tests in `application-shortcuts.test.cjs` and `project-manager.test.cjs`; verified live in NW.js.
- **Encounter and battle transition shaders land where they aim.** `reactor_mv_compat.js`: `installVec2Compat` runs after the compat filter's `UniformGroup` is built and gives every scalar `vec2<f32>` uniform a get/set on `group.uniforms` that adopts the assigned value (`adoptVec2`: arrays and typed arrays gain `.x/.y` aliases onto `[0]/[1]`, `{x, y}` objects gain `[0]/[1]` and a `length`, a bare `{}` is seeded from the value it replaces, a number becomes `[n, n]`, `null` keeps the value, an expando written before adoption is folded into the slot). Why: v8's sync generator tests the group's *construction-time* value for `.x` to pick the point parser and caches the result per group signature and program; pixi-filters v5 classes call `super()` with no uniforms, so the test sees the seeded typed array and every later component write is invisible. `pixi_compat.js`: `PIXI.TexturePool.getOptimalTexture` is wrapped so a request matching `Graphics._canvas` (read lazily; the editor has none) gets an exactly sized texture under a negative pool key, with a stale-size pooled texture destroyed on canvas resize; every other request takes v8's native power-of-two path. Tests: two vec2 cases in `legacy-filter-apply-compat.test.cjs` (whose fake `UniformGroup` now normalises structures in place like v8) and `pixi-fullscreen-texture-pool-compat.test.cjs`.
- **Effekseer animations show again in VisuStella battles, and two more PIXI 8 gaps close from a user's debug report.** `pixi_compat.js` wraps `PIXI.Sprite.prototype.updateTransform` on v8 so the v5 no-args call (VisuStella CoreEngine's battler projection, `targetDistortionSpritePosition` in BattleCore) refreshes the local transform and returns instead of throwing on `opts.x`; the `(opts)` form passes through, and the wrap is Sprite-only on purpose (Container/Window/Tilemap plugin chains rely on the throw skipping their post-super work, and `Window.updateTransform` already branches on v8, so the old "removed wrap" note was stale). `Sprite_Animation.renderActive` reports the first `_doEffekseerDraw` throw once (`_effekseerDrawWarned`) instead of swallowing every frame. `reactor_mv_compat.js` `buildUniformStructures` seeds unset scalar array uniforms (`f32` → `Float32Array(N)`, `i32` → `Int32Array(N)`, lone `int` → 0) because `UniformGroup`'s `getDefaultUniformValue` returns the number 0 for `f32` whatever the size and nothing for `i32`, which reached `gl.uniform1fv` as a scalar and threw (DA_HeatDistortion's `sourceX/Y/R[10]` in Global mode). Tests: `pixi-sprite-update-transform-compat.test.cjs` and a seeding case in `legacy-filter-apply-compat.test.cjs`.
- **The Audio Player loops at LOOPSTART/LOOPLENGTH.** `AudioPlayer` drove looping through `HTMLMediaElement.loop`, which repeats the whole file; the tags only reached the game's `WebAudio`. New `src/utils/AudioLoopTags.js` ports the runtime's readers (OGG/FLAC vorbis comments, ID3v2 TXXX, WAV `smpl`) over a growing prefix read through `RREncryptedAssets.readAssetBytes` (a WAV whose `smpl` trails the data is read whole), returning `{start, end}` seconds. Each channel keeps `loopWanted`/`loopPoints`; `setChannelLoop` is the only place the element's `loop` flag is set, and it stays off while loop points exist. A per-frame `watchLoopPoints` wraps `currentTime` from the loop end back to the start keeping the overshoot, `ended` wraps a loop that runs to the file end, and `seeked` disarms the wrap past the end so the tail can be auditioned. Verified in NW.js against an ffmpeg-tagged fixture (wraps 2.99 → 1.03 s, never past the end) and by parsing the stock MZ RTP battle themes to their known points.
- **The Enemies note textarea keeps its dragged height.** `DatabaseEnemyEditor` gave the note `flex: 1` (basis `0%`) inside a stretched column, so the inline `height` the resize handle writes was ignored by the flex algorithm (measured: 400px drag, 199px result); `flex: 1 1 auto` takes it, as every other page's note already did.
- **Actor face icons show in the database list on Windows.** `DatabaseEditorUI.applyListIcon` built `encodeURI('file://' + path)` without converting backslashes, so Windows paths became `file:///E:/%5CGame%20Dev%5C…` — `ERR_INVALID_URL` on every actor row, whether or not the face file existed. Backslashes are now converted before encoding and `#` is escaped, matching the sibling builders in the file (`database-list-icon-windows-url.test.cjs`). Separately, 161 `console.log` progress lines across the tileset/animation/weapon/armor/system editors, EventManager, ProjectManager, and the database shell moved to `console.debug` (DevTools' Verbose level), and the runtime's `Reactor3D: 3D scene built.` report did the same.
- **Three Demo tracks no longer jump to the start when you seek.** The cover-art restoration pass remuxed audio with stream copy, and on Battle Of The Abyss, Darkstream Runner, and Pixel Party the remux wrote Ogg pages whose granule position carried a stray 2^32 samples: a four-minute song reported a duration of 24 hours 55 minutes, so seeking a minute ahead landed "past the end" and the player looped back to zero (or stopped with loop off), identically on desktop and web because the corruption is in the files. The three tracks are regenerated from their originals through the optimizer's single-pass encode (art embedded in the same pass, no copy remux), verified in a live browser: exact durations, and a seek to 60% keeps playing. The optimizer now reads the Ogg clock — last granule position over the Vorbis sample rate — of input and output on every re-encode and refuses a result whose duration drifts more than 2%, so this class of corruption can never ship silently again.
- **The web editor's 3D suite works: previews render, clips list, rigs bind.** Four layers deep: the 3D library loader's web branch checked `RPGReactorHost` while the host registers as `RPGReactorWebHost` — one word that kept three.js from ever loading in a browser, invisible to the suite because its test stubbed the same wrong name; the test now stubs the real global. Editor model reads (DB previews, embedded clips, the picker, rig weight binaries) go through a new async asset reader — synchronous on desktop, a fetch of the served file on web — because a synchronous XHR fallback deadlocks against the service worker (it reads the same IndexedDB store the page writes mid-transaction). The `3d/**/model.json` sidecars join the web build's preloaded files so synchronous sidecar reads keep working, and a `Buffer` polyfill covering the editor's exact API surface unblocks every binary writer: rig binaries, PNG and zip exports, the Forge generators. Verified in a served web build driven end to end: the Database 3D preview renders, Carol lists her 20 embedded clips, and Bind rig on both actors solves, writes model.json + model.rig.bin through the web filesystem, reads them back, and persists to browser storage — matching desktop behavior exactly.
- **Two more PIXI 8 compat gaps closed from live plugin reports.** Hendrix_Animation_Solution crashed calling `PIXI.utils.string2hex` for its bloom tint — the v5 color helpers (string2hex, hex2string, hex2rgb, rgb2hex) are reimplemented in the utils shim. And action combat could crash the whole GL pass ("reading 'addressModeU'") when a plugin destroyed a bitmap mid-animation on a non-Sprite renderable the SpritePipe guard doesn't see — GlTextureSystem now substitutes the empty texture for any dead source at bind, the last line of defense under every render pipe. A live audit of every `PIXI.*` path the Hendrix and Haven plugin packs touch (24 distinct) resolves clean against the shimmed runtime, and a scripted fresh playthrough of Haven — nine maps of intro, fast-forwarded dialogue, a battle fought and exited — runs with a silent console.
- **Loading a save from a map-based title no longer risks a soft lock.** Haven's title screen is itself a map scene; DataManager.loadGame replaces $gameMap instantly while the old scene still updates through its fade-out frames, so every plugin sprite keyed by map id (GALV layer graphics) read the wrong map's store and threw a TypeError per frame — a storm that stalled the scene transition and left the first battle soft-locked with no visible error. Spriteset_Map now stamps the map it was built for and holds still once $gameMap moves on; verified on a real Haven save: clean load, and a battle started through the engine's own Battle Processing command reaches Scene_Battle and plays turns with a silent console.
- **A quieter console.** PIXI's one-shot deprecation nags for the v5 Graphics API (beginFill, drawRect, endFill...) are burned off at compat load with warn muted — MZ plugins use that API forever, the shims work, and nobody can act on the nag — and the runtime's leftover Effekseer overlay/lifecycle and animation-anchor debug diagnostics are removed from the play path (the console-invoked helpers like $reactorAnimWatch stay).
- **PIXI 8's new `origin` accessor no longer hijacks engine bookkeeping.** Newer PIXI Containers claim `origin` as a live transform input; MZ semantics store scroll bookkeeping Points there (Tilemap, TilingSprite, Window, Weather — and MOG_Weather_EX, which warned "Setting both a pivot and origin" sixty times per map). Harmless while nothing rotates (origin cancels out of an identity transform) but wrong the moment a weather sprite spins with a large origin. The accessor joins `name` in the pre-boot deletion list, so assignments become plain instance properties again; verified against the Demo (plain origin, live scroll values, clean console) and a real Haven save (weather plugin active, zero warnings).
- **Benign window-chrome churn no longer warns every playtest.** The render guard's report for a live sprite whose texture source died distinguished nothing about the source — but a destroyed CANVAS source is the churn pattern plugins use for drawn window chrome (SelectableWindow destroys and recreates its gradient bitmap every geometry pass; the part sprites hold the dead canvas for one frame before reassignment). The skip covers that frame and the plugin heals itself, so the canvas case now reports on console.debug (visible at Verbose); a destroyed sprite, nulled GPU data, or a dead file-backed texture is a real leak and stays a warning.
- **The Database 3D panel behaves on first open.** On a fresh editor, `_readEmbeddedClips` ran before the 3D libraries had ever loaded — `Reactor3D` was still undefined, so a model's embedded clips came back empty and every clip rule looked unresolved until the model was visited a second time (the libraries load inside the preview draw; the clips now read after it). And on rigged models the Animations header packed a differently-styled Motions… chip beside Add and Delete into the fixed-width panel, pushing Delete past the dialog edge where it clipped; Motions… now sits on its own full-width row below the header, styled like every other button.
- **The web editor renders 3D.** Database model previews were black in the browser — the running game was fine — because editor previews read model bytes, sidecars, and rig binaries through synchronous `fs` calls the web build only honored for preloaded data files. The web filesystem shim now fetches any bundled file on demand through a synchronous request (cached after first read), which lights up the Database 3D section, the actor/item mini previews, and the model picker with no call-site changes.

## [0.98.3] - 2026-08-24

### Added

- **Database > 3D carves model regions into named parts and poses them with a direct-manipulation card.** The preview's tool strip holds Orbit, box Select, and Pivot; the right button or Ctrl always orbits. With Orbit active, hovering any poseable part boxes it and a stationary click opens the edit card — carved parts and reader-named parts (a GLB wheel) alike, picked by raycast through the part ancestry. Select drags a marquee and every triangle the rectangle touches joins the part — a vertex inside the box, the box poking into a big triangle, or an edge crossing all count, where centre-only testing let a visibly covered jaw plate slip away — through the model, so a jaw's far side comes with the near side, with Alt-drag removing (same touch test) and a floating count/Apply/Cancel bar; rules freeze while selecting so the mesh cannot slide out from under the marquee, selection always runs against the uncarved geometry so triangle indices count over the source, and Apply flows straight into posing the new part. The card is the whole editor and never leaves: it docks whenever the model has parts, its header carries a target dropdown (Whole model included) beside undo/redo, its × folds it to a small button, and a **Type** select switches the motion — **Pose** (Rotate / Offset / Scale tabs of color-coded X/Y/Z sliders), **Swing** / **Spin** / **Bob** (axis chips plus degree, speed, per-tile, and amount sliders), or an embedded **Clip** — every control driven on `input` so the model moves live through a synthetic always-on rule in the real engine, while every OTHER ambient animation stands still so nothing fights the hand (explicitly played actions still run; releasing the card resumes them). The numeric rule form on the right is gone — the panel keeps only the Parts and Animations lists, and clicking any animation opens it on the card. The card also carries a logarithmic **Duration** slider (0.1–10 s, the ease period in frames underneath), a **Play when** trigger select, an **At the end** control (Return to rest / **Stay posed**, the pose rule's `hold` flag), **Preview** (plays the pose once via a timed action rule — in-and-out, or in-and-hold for a held pose, the editing hold taking over seamlessly), double-click-to-reset per slider, and **Save as animation** / **Update animation**; an edited animation's saved copy is filtered out of playback so nothing plays twice, and a fresh card always defaults to On demand rather than inheriting the last rule's trigger. The card keeps state: working poses persist per part so reselecting resumes the sliders where they were left (a part with exactly one saved pose opens it for editing), each slider gesture stashes on its first tick and commits one undo step on release, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y (and header arrows) walk a per-part undo/redo trail, and Escape closes the card ahead of the dialog. In the runtime, a held on-demand pose latches: the action eases it in over one period (`modelRuleDuration` is `period`, not `period×2`) and `binding.latch` keeps it there after the action expires — until another held pose claims the same part, which eases the previous one home; transient in-out poses overlay a held pose without releasing it, and the sim bar gains a Reset pose button that clears the latches. A part stores `{ name, pivot, meshes: { meshIndex: [[tri,count],…] } }` in `model.json` (foreign sidecar keys preserved); definitions may overlap, and the carve treats overlap as nesting — triangles are grouped by the exact set of parts claiming them, and each piece carries every claiming name as ancestry, most specific (fewest triangles) first with its own pivot, so a cannon selected inside a turret answers its own rules about its own hinge while riding every turret rule (the previous first-definition-wins partition silently carved such a nested part to zero triangles: no box, dead sliders — found on the real tank, not by the single-mesh tests). In playback, a mesh's contributions compose by that same ancestry rather than rule order — ancestors' turns apply before a child's own motion, so a fired cannon recoils along the turned barrel whether the fire rule sits before the turn in the array or rides at the end of the chain as the card's working copy (authoring-order composition slid the recoil along the model's original axis the moment the orders crossed); `Reactor3D.carveModelParts` splits those triangle groups into sibling meshes at instance time — attributes shared, remainder reindexed, never mutating the template — registered exactly like reader-named parts and carrying the source mesh's ancestry so old rules still match, which is how the same carve serves the editor preview and the running game (`syncCharacterModels` loads one sidecar for rules and parts, stat-ing it on disk first so a model without one logs nothing). Pivots are movable fulcrums for every part, named or carved: `pivots: { "<part>": [x,y,z] }` overrides in `model.json` (model space, converted to mesh-local by `Reactor3D.applyPivotOverrides` after the carve, in the editor and the game sync alike), authored from a Pivot row on the card (bounds presets: center and the six faces), by clicking the model with the Pivot tool, or by grabbing the axes gizmo itself and sliding it in the camera plane — the part re-hinges live on every change. The card's target dropdown retargets the animation being edited (values intact; a deleted part's animation shows its target marked `?` for re-aiming) while viewport clicks keep switching context, and a click on the canvas lets an open dropdown dismiss itself instead of preventDefault pinning the popup open. The new **pose** rule type eases to an authored rotation, translation, and per-axis scale (`resize`, applied about the part pivot; mesh base scale now round-trips through the animation compose) and back as its trigger holds and releases — action poses play in-and-out over `period×2×cycles`, with the blend persisted so a released trigger eases home instead of snapping. Renaming a part renames it in every rule that targets it; a part with no triangles yet says so on the card instead of silently ignoring the sliders. A precision-and-state pass hardened the flow: the marquee is occlusion-aware by default — every projected triangle carries per-corner view depth, a screen-space grid answers "does anything nearer cover this centroid" (`triangleDepthAt`, epsilon-tolerant so coplanar neighbours never self-occlude), and a **Through** toggle in the select bar restores far-side selection; each drag and Clear pushes a selection undo step (select-bar arrow or Ctrl+Z, Escape cancels the mode). Selection-highlight overlays are flagged `__reactorOverlay` and excluded from `carveTargetMeshes`, which had let a full-canvas box count more triangles than the model holds and could shift mesh numbering. Rule suppression no longer filters the playback array — blends and latches key by index, and the re-keying scrambled latched poses — suppressed rules are swapped for inert stand-ins in place; the sim bar's Play for the rule open on the card previews the card's current values instead of hitting a dead slot, and the card's Reset also clears latches so one button always means "model at rest". A fresh card no longer inherits the previous rule's trigger. A polish pass tuned the feel against a real multi-part tank: Preview zeroes its own blend slot and releases held copies of its pose first, so it visibly plays from rest even while the card holds the pose at full strength (a held pose previously had nowhere to go and Preview looked dead); opening an animation releases its latch — the card takes manual control and nothing pops back up on deselect; the card's second button is **Clear** (zero the sliders, undoable) with latch release living on Preview and the sim bar; a **＋** beside Save detaches from the rule being edited so a part with one animation can get a second; the pivot gizmo grew, renders through the mesh (it used to be buried inside the part), drags from any tool with a move cursor, and is anchored to the part's owning mesh — its position re-derives every frame through freshly updated matrices, where a one-shot placement read a stale (unscaled) transform right after a rebuild and otherwise froze at whatever pose the part held at that instant, which made the fulcrum appear to jump between reselects; Add part cancels a running selection session so a new part never inherits the old triangles; and the highlight boxes are refit every frame instead of hanging where a pose once stood. On-demand rules carry `effects: [{ at, se|animation|flash }]` in the sidecar — `at` a 0–1 fraction of the play. The sync loop fires each effect once as the action clock crosses its mark (`modelEffectsToFire`, pure and window-tested): SEs through `AudioManager`, database animations through `$gameTemp.requestAnimation` on the character (MV sheets and Effekseer both ride the stock pipeline), screen flashes through `$gameScreen`, and model flashes by tinting the instance's own materials — `cloneModelTemplate` now clones materials per instance so one tank flashes alone, rebuilding `userData.baseColor` as a real Colour because Material.clone's JSON pass degrades it to a hex number (which read as undefined channels and poisoned the flash; `updateModelFlash` also self-heals any degraded base). The card grows an Effects section on on-demand animations: per-row timing slider, ＋ Sound through the shared `RRAudioPickerModal`, ＋ Animation through `AnimationPickerModal`, ＋ Flash with an inline target/colour/strength/duration editor; the editor preview plays SEs via the project's audio files and shows model and background flashes at the authored moment (database animations play in game). The animation picker opened from the card now passes the section's own project shim — the global manager answered a different shape, `project` stayed null, and `startPlayback` bailed to a black canvas for sprite AND Effekseer animations alike; the picker's list also joins the `audio-scroll` accent-pill scrollbar treatment and its footer wears the header's `--color-bg-panel` (both improvements reach the Skills/Items/Weapons pages that share the modal). The Animations list highlight mirrors the card everywhere (editRule/select/deselect/＋/Add all sync `selectedRule`), every card-filling action clears `_cardCollapsed`, the panel's Add opens a neutral motionless animation (its old whole-model swing default rocked the entire model — invisibly, when the card was collapsed), the ＋ button reads "＋ New", and both latch-release paths (`_releaseLatches`, the sim bar's Reset pose) also cancel a matching in-flight `_sim.action`, because a hold whose action window was still open — Turn-Turret-Right runs 600 frames — re-latched on the next frame and quietly undid its own release. After a Preview of a return-to-rest pose the stage keeps the ending — the held working pose stands suppressed until the next slider touch, because it used to ease back in the moment the preview finished, which read as the cannon firing twice and "resting at the end instead of the beginning" (found on the owner's Fire Canon recoil; every edit path re-engages the pose via `_syncWorkRule`). Beyond the scripted flows, a seeded monkey harness (`scratchpad/db3d-monkey.mjs`) drives hundreds of random-order UI actions against invariants (no exceptions, card/state/sidecar consistency, finite transforms) on the plant and on the 35-part tank, and a directed scenario (`db3d-two-parts.mjs`) proves two parts' animations stay fully independent through play, resume, update, and delete. Verified over CDP in a live NW.js editor: docked card before any pick, occlusion counts (visible-only 3,428 vs 8,184 through on the 8,222-triangle plant), selection undo, carve on monster-plant, slider rotate/scale moving the carved mesh, ambient sway freezing while a different target is edited and resuming on release, preview timing, held poses latching and easing home, undo/redo/resume, sidecar write, sim-bar replay, and viewport picking. All new UI phrases ship curated in the 17 non-English locales.

- **Four rig templates, a preset motion library, and keyframed animations complete the in-editor rigging suite.** The Rig tool's bar gains a template picker: **Humanoid** (17 bones), **Quadruped** (18 — spine chain, tail, four three-bone legs), **Plant/Tree** (base–trunk–crown, wind-ready), and **Vehicle** (body plus four point-like wheel bones, so wheels rig by dropping a marker on each hub instead of marquee-carving through geometry). The weight solver's side gate grew a second axis with real thresholds — a quadruped's front legs stay off its rear legs, and a spine marker nudged slightly off centre can no longer gate the torso against half the body. Swing and bob rules take a **phase** offset, which is all a walk cycle is: the new **Motions…** button on a rigged model opens the **Preset Motions** library — Walk, Run, Breathe, Wave, Take a Bow, Nod, Shake Head, Sit, Overhead Strike for humanoids; Walk, Idle Sway, Pounce for quadrupeds; Wind Sway and Rustle for plants; Roll and Bounce for vehicles — and applying one drops plain, individually editable rules into the Animations list (reapplying replaces that preset's rules; hand-authored ones are untouched). Gestures ride the other new mechanic, **keyframes**: an on-demand pose can carry a timeline of captured slider stops the action interpolates through — rest, through each stop, back to rest — authored on the card with a per-stop time slider, capture, and delete, and multi-bone gestures compose by sharing one action name. The library also carries the action repertoire a game actually needs: **Jump** (real root motion through keyframes — crouch dip, launch, hang, land, all on the whole model while the limbs tuck), **Swim** and **Float** (a swimmer's whole mode: prone stroke cycle while moving, treading drift while idle — a model carries Walk or Swim on the moving trigger, not both), **Slash**, **Thrust**, and **Overhead Strike** for melee (torso coil driving the arm through the arc), and the held stances **Aim Rifle**, **Aim Pistol**, **Dual Wield**, and **Guard** — fire one and it stays until **Lower Arms** (or another held stance on the same bones) takes over, which is the existing latch mechanic doing exactly what it was built for. The sim bar shows one Play button per action name rather than one per rule, so a six-bone stance is one button. Verified live twice over: the Demo engineer walks with legs counter-swinging through 61° and a keyframed Wave that peaks at exactly its authored 140° and returns to rest; and a Meshy-exported 135 MB, 1.62-million-vertex character loads in 3 s, binds in 6 s, and walks in the preview at 185 fps on GPU skinning. All new text ships in the 17 non-English locales.

- **Size = largest dimension; camera easing.** The map sync's `span = max(extent.x, extent.z)` (footprint) became `max(x, y, z)` in BOTH the visual scale and the collision footprint (they must agree) — Fleagus (0.90 wide) and reduced Carol (0.66 wide), both 1.70 tall at size 2, rendered 3.8 vs 5.1 tiles tall under footprint normalization; both now stand exactly 2.00 (verified in-game as leader + follower). The car/bike/plant are wider than tall, so their sizing is untouched. Preview jitter: a frame-gap probe measured zero drops (worst 7ms at 596k tris) during orbit/zoom — the "jitter" was discrete stepping. Database3DEditor and ModelGraphicPicker gain `_viewGoal` + per-frame exponential easing (~70ms time constant, frame-rate independent); wheel steps at 1.15× per notch into the goal; the picker's face-framing also glides to its crop. Verified: goal jumps 4→1.73 on six notches while the view settles smoothly to it.

- **Binary weights sidecar (`model.rig.bin`).** Layout: u32 magic "RRWB" / version / meshCount, then per mesh u32 meshIndex + u32 vertexCount + count×4 indices + count×4 weights (the existing top-4 Uint8 quantization, no base64). `ModelRigger.encodeWeightsBinary/decodeWeightsBinary/buildRigBinary` (pure, dual-export); `Reactor3D.decodeRigWeightsBinary` is the parity-pinned runtime mirror. `bindRig` builds the binary form (JSON carries `weightsFile`, never bytes; the in-memory decode rides `customRig.weightsBin`, stripped by a JSON replacer on save); `saveRig` writes/deletes the bin beside model.json; `loadSidecar` and `loadModelSidecar` read it back and attach `weightsBin` (the pointer is a bare filename — path-like values refused); `readModelRig` accepts attached buffers or legacy base64 identically (parity-tested). Verified live end to end: bind → 7KB model.json + 242KB bin, disk-reload reads 2 meshes/17 bones back, and the running game skins the player through the binary path with all Walk rules live.

- **Editor previews on the worker, preview warm-up, folder glyph/state sync.** `Reactor3D.readModelAsync(buffer, ext, baseUrl, texture)` wraps the worker-or-sync dance once; `Database3DEditor._loadTemplate`, `ModelGraphicPicker._drawPreview`, and `EventPageEditor._renderModelPreview` all await it (each already had generation guards). `_rebuildInstance` runs `renderer.compile` + `initTexture` right after attaching the instance — shader compilation and texture upload were trickling through the first seconds of orbiting as one-frame hitches ("smooths after a time"). Folder-list fix: `renderModelList` resolved the auto-selection AFTER painting, so `selectModel`'s folder-open seed landed post-paint — the header drew ▸ while the state said open, making the first click a no-op close ("click twice" reports); the selection now resolves and seeds before the rows paint. Verified live: first paint shows the selection's folder expanded, single-click toggles both ways, `_parseWorker` live in the editor, reduced Carol's template loads through it and plays Walking.

- **Deploy dialogs rebuild on language change; full audio-quality range.** BuildManager and DistEditorManager stamp `_builtLanguage` in `setupModal` (now idempotent — it removes the previous modal element first) and `open()` rebuilds when the current language differs and no worker is running — the tt()-baked markup was frozen at construction while every phrase already sat translated in the deep catalog. The quality select lists 1–10 (descriptive anchors kept at 3/5/7/10, bare digits between — digits need no translation) and `DeploymentAssetPreferences` snaps to the full range instead of [3,5,7,10].

- **Map model preloading, GPU warm-up, worker-parsed GLB.** `collectMapModelSpecs` gathers a map's full model list (event sidecar pages + every actor's character slot, deduped by cache key; gated on `isMap3D`, not the loaded-runtime checks — cold boots haven't loaded THREE yet); `preloadMapModels` loads them all behind the loading fade; `Scene_Map` wrappers (END of reactor_scenes.js) gate `isReady` on the preload with an 8s fail-open timer. `warmLoadedTemplates` adds unwarmed cached templates to a throwaway scene for one `renderer.compile` pass + `initTexture` per texture — called at Scene_Map.start and from the sync loop for late arrivals (Set-guarded no-op steady-state). GLB parsing moves off-thread: `reactorSplitGlb` + `reactorDecodeGlbImages` live in reactor_3d.js, get `toString()`-assembled into a Blob-URL worker (the runtime stays one module — the architecture tests rejected a separate worker file, correctly; a Blob worker also serves the editor), transfer the file buffer in, and return parsed JSON + `ImageBitmap`s (decoded with glTF's unflipped/unpremultiplied orientation); `buildGlbTemplate`/`_loadGlbTexture` accept the bitmaps and wrap them directly as textures. Any worker failure transfers the buffer back to the synchronous parser; non-GLB formats never leave it. Verified live: the map gate holds while five models load, all five instances stand with objects one second into gameplay (previously 10–15s of visible pop-in), OBJ still loads, zero errors.

- **Event editor chrome pass.** The autonomous-movement Route… button joins `rr-btn-chip` (it painted itself in the panel background with no hover); the event Name input and Note textarea take the theme's surface/border tokens (the name field was a browser-default white box); the variable condition reads `Variable  [picker] ≥ [value]` — the ≥ moved between the name and the value where it reads naturally, the label's own ">=:" suffix dropped, and the value field is themed; the commands column's header wears `database-section-header` instead of blending into the background.

- **Head-on picker camera, stale-transform animation anchor, deferred thumbnails.** ModelGraphicPicker's default view is `{yaw: 0, pitch: 18}` — the 25-degree default meant "looks straight at me" authored a −25-ish yaw the map faithfully played back (found via a per-frame trace: direction resolved 2 everywhere, yaw constant −0.476 rad = the stored −27°; the owner's Fleagus binding was corrected). `Sprite_Animation.targetSpritePosition` falls back to `parent.worldTransform.apply(sprite.x, sprite.y)` when the target sprite is invisible or its own worldTransform yields non-finite points — v8 never updates transforms for sprites that skip the render pass, and a model event's hidden stand-in is exactly that, so animations anchored to wherever the transform froze (verified: anchor now returns the sprite's exact projected position beside the plant). `renderModelList` schedules `_fillThumbnails` after the paint and the fill yields a macrotask before each uncached render — first-time GLB load+clone+render chunks were swallowing folder-toggle clicks. `docs/demo-missing-se.md` lists the 120 stock SE names the animation database references that audio/se no longer holds.

- **General-panel mini model previews, in-list (None), stale-sprite skip.** `attachRow` gains `previewHost`/`thumbnail`: a cached render of the bound model under the icon column, live-measured to the icon preview's exact footprint for symmetry (weapon/armor/item editors pass `.db-general-grid > div`; the row itself moves into `.db-form` so it aligns with the fields); the shared `modelThumbnail(reactor3dEditor, spec)` helper now lives in Database3DBindings (exported — its first ship missed the api object and the throw blanked the whole weapon panel; thumbnail calls are wrapped so a provider failure can never take the detail down again). `RRPickerIndex.createBrowser` gains `leadingItem` — an action row pinned atop the file list — and `showImagePicker`'s (None) uses it instead of a row above the search box. The face and enemy-battler callbacks call `refreshListIcon`, so clearing a face updates the actor list immediately. Runtime: `Sprite_Character.updateBitmap` wrapper (end of reactor_sprites.js) skips the 2D sheet load when `characterModelSpec` answers — a 3D-bound actor's leftover characterName 404'd the map. Verified live: Short Sword's model beside its icon, row aligned, clean Demo boot with zero load errors, player model up.

- **Facing marks optional + 3D toggle beside the button.** The markless default in `applyEventModelPose` (yaw = dir8Yaw + authored) already equals the glTF export convention (front = +Z, toward the camera at rest) — now pinned by a contract test and surfaced in the picker: a `.model-face-status` line says marks are optional, and a **Clear marks** button (visible only with marks set) resets `selectedFaces` to the export default. `decorateSlot` moved the 3D checkbox from an absolute top corner into a flex row with the graphic's change button — no label padding tricks, titles single-line. Three new phrases ship in the 17 locales.

- **Actor-page 3D perf, (None) graphics, centred labels, trim fixes.** The slot thumbnail provider consults `reactor3dEditor._thumbs` (the 3D section's own data-URL cache) before rendering — cloning+rendering a large model per detail re-render was the typing/click lag — with a one-shot cache refresh at 1.8s for late-decoding embedded textures. `showImagePicker` gains `options.allowNone`: a (None) row above the file browser that calls back with `''` (character/face/SV/enemy battler pickers opt in), and those pickers' empty-folder alerts are gone — an empty `sv_actors` no longer blocks clearing a 3D-bound battler. `decorateSlot` pads the box label on both sides so it centres. `dist-editor-worker`'s model trim collects names from `Database.r3d.json` (event sidecars alone would strip bound actor/enemy models from web builds) and recurses into organization folders, trimming per model folder and pruning emptied parents — flat-walk deleted a whole nested folder over one unused member. Demo reorganized into `Actors/Enemies/Vehicles/Weapons`; `Map001.r3d.json` references remapped to the new paths.

- **Nested model folders.** `ModelGraphicPicker.listModels` walks `3d/` recursively (depth-capped, skipping `source`/`textures`), naming each model by its forward-slash path under `3d/`; the source-file match compares the path's last segment. `splitModelRef` accepts slashed segments (backslashes normalized) while refusing empty, `.`, and `..` segments, so notes and sidecars stay jailed inside `3d/`. Every URL and path builder already composed `3d/<name>/…` by concatenation, so nested names flow through loading, sidecars, thumbnails, bindings, and deployment unchanged. Both lists render folders as collapsible groups: the DB 3D Models list groups by first segment (open-state per folder, the selection's folder held open, searches expand matches, indented short names, thumbnails fill per visible row), and `RRPickerIndex.createBrowser` gains an opt-in `folders` mode — folder sections ahead of the letter sections, same toggle rules — used by the model picker (the shared item factory was refactored, other pickers unchanged). Verified live on the Demo's `Weapons/Sword_Fleagus`: collapsed by default, toggles open in both lists, selects, and renders.

- **Per-slot actor 3D (character / face / battler) and gait-specific triggers.** `Database.r3d.json` actor entries become `{ character, face, battler }` slots (a flat legacy entry reads as its character slot and migrates on the next slot write; `Database3DBindings.get/set` take an optional slot, `Reactor3D.actorSlotSpec(id, slot)` reads them, `databaseModelSpec('actors', id)` stays the character slot for the map). `decorateSlot` mounts a corner 3D checkbox on each of the actor page's graphic boxes: bound, the box retitles, swaps its 2D canvas for a 140px model thumbnail (rendered twice — a first render can precede embedded-texture decode and bake a silhouette), and its button opens the model picker in capture phase; unbound, the original 2D flow is untouched. The face slot's picker adds `{framing: true}` — Zoom and Height sliders stored as `spec.view`, with the preview camera steering to the exact crop.
