The Note textarea no longer derives its height from a stretched parent and gained `resize: vertical`. `map-properties-layout.test.cjs` pins each control appearing exactly once, the sizing rules, and the packing behavior.

- Standing geometry in 3D is billboarded rather than fixed. `Reactor3D.Geometry.build` now emits cut-outs into their own groups, flagged `billboard`, whose `positions` hold the object's anchor repeated per vertex and whose `offsets` hold each corner in tiles; `Reactor3D.billboardMaterial` assembles the quad in the vertex shader on the camera's own right and up axes, read back into world space from the first two columns of `viewMatrix`. The up axis is `mix(worldUp, cameraUp, Reactor3D.BILLBOARD_TILT)`, and `BILLBOARD_TILT` is 0 — upright, yaw only, matching what `faceCamera` has always done for the event sprites. Any lean displaces a cut-out's art horizontally from its anchor by `height * |up.xz|`, which swings with the azimuth: at 0.75 and a 45 degree camera, a six-tile object's top sits 3.4 tiles out and moves 4.8 tiles through a quarter turn, which reads as the object drifting off its own cells and as everything sitting above the ground. At 0 that displacement is identically zero. The degenerate overhead case that leaning was meant to solve is handled by clamping `orbit` to 72 degrees instead. `polygonOffset` handles the coplanar case that creates, where a flat-lying cut-out shares a plane with the ground under it. This replaced a fixed south-facing plane plus a second plane crossing it at right angles, which existed only so an object did not vanish edge-on and which showed as a seam through the art at intermediate angles. Hinging at the ★ flag was tried first and does not work: on the Infernis gantry only the top of three rows is starred, since that is the only row that must overlap a character behind it, so ★ is a lower bound on height and not a partition of the prop.

- A3 and the A4 wall rows derive as `CLASS_SCENERY` rather than `CLASS_UPRIGHT`. As cut-outs they were built per column on the run's southern face, so on Map564 (a 79x111 labyrinth, 4,800 A3 cells) the overhead view showed bare floor with slivers where the maze should be, and obliquely a wall block eight cells deep stacked into an eight-unit tower. Raised, the walled area is solid, the corridors are carved from it, and the vertical faces come from the wall pass — which takes `stack[0]`, the wall autotile itself, since a dungeon paints walls on layer 0. The fixed-plane path in `Geometry.build` is kept for a tile an author classifies upright by hand.

- Declared 3D objects in `Tilesets.r3d.json`: an `objects` list per tileset, each `{tile, w, h, roles}`, where `tile` is the top-left of a rectangle on the sheet and `roles` is `w * h` characters of `S` (stands) or `F` (lies flat). `Tileset3DClass` gains `objectAt`, `roleOf`, `defineObject`, `clearObject` and `cycleRole`; `Reactor3D` mirrors `objectAt`/`tileRole` and `uprightPredicate` returns false for an `F` tile, so a role overrides a class. `Geometry.uprightObjects` takes a `declaredAt` lookup and groups declared tiles by instance origin — a cell's map position minus its position within the object — which is exact, before falling back to the adjacency guess for anything undeclared. A synthetic case shows why: tiles 8-9 and 10-11 painted side by side group as one four-wide object by adjacency and as two by declaration. The generator seeds a declaration per solid rectangular block of standing prop art. Shift-click two corners in the 3D Shape editor to declare, shift-click a declared tile to clear, alt-click to flip a role; declared objects draw as a yellow outline with a bar on flat cells. Coverage in `tileset-3d-class.test.cjs` and `reactor-3d-geometry.test.cjs`.

- 3D authoring is tool-based and applied by dragging a rectangle. `tile3dTools`/`tile3dToolbar` render a palette into the key panel, `attachTile3DDrag` owns the pointer on every sheet canvas, and `applyTile3DTool` applies the selected tool across `dragBounds`. `handleCompactCanvasClick` no longer handles `tile3d` at all — a click is a 1x1 drag. Cycling on click left no way to inspect a tile without altering it, and counting corners was the wrong shape for declaring a rectangle. The drag scales the pointer by `canvas.width / rect.width`, since the sheet is laid out at a different size from its backing store. `select` changes nothing, `object` declares, `erase` undeclares, `role` decides once for the whole rectangle rather than toggling per tile so a mixed selection is not scrambled.

- A5 is treated as a picture sheet rather than an autotile throughout: `Tileset3DClass.isPictureTile` and `Reactor3D.isPictureTile` admit 1-2047, `sheetCell`/`tileAtCell` address A5 as an eight-wide grid at `setNumber` 4 with no half-sheet split, `Geometry.samePicture` and `uprightObjects` group it with B-G, and the fixed-plane wall path narrows to `>= 2048`. The old `< 1536` cutoff put A5 with the autotiles, so an A5 wall could not be declared as an object at all.

- `previewArtSource` reads an autotile's cell from `cachedBaseCanvas` — the entry in `imageCache` the tab canvas is composed from — rather than the tab canvas itself, which has the flag overlay drawn onto it; the preview was showing the class chevron as part of the art. `drawTile3DPreview` derives its cell size from the object's extent instead of a fixed 22px.

- `applyTile3DTool` refuses a 1x1 `object` drag over a tile that is already declared, refuses `object`/`erase`/`role` on autotile indices with a reason on the console, and sets `selected3dRect` to the declared object's rectangle when the selection falls inside one — the overlay draws that rather than the clicked cell. Coverage in `tileset-3d-authoring.test.cjs`, including that a class painted on an A tab lands on the kind's base id and reads back from every shape of it.

- An upright object's anchor is the centre of its footprint in x *and* z, not the middle of its southern row. The anchor is the axis the billboard rotates about, so an off-centre one makes the object orbit that point: a six-deep object swung two and a half tiles around its own front edge through a quarter turn. Height stays separate — `base` is still `surfaceAt` at the southern row, the ground the object faces — and conflating the two is what made the southern row look correct for a while. `drawTile3DPreview` had the same bug and the same fix.

- `Geometry.build` computes a `roofLift` pass before `surfaceAt`: connected regions of A3/A4 art that are not themselves raised, and that touch a raised cell, take that cell's height. Walls raise into a mass through `CLASS_SCENERY`, and the roof behind them is ordinary flat terrain, so a building rendered as a block with its roof lying on the ground beside it. Coverage in `reactor-3d-geometry.test.cjs`.

- `drawTile3DPreview` projects a slanted, gridded ground plane that rotates with the drag, and `previewArtSource` reads an autotile's art from the rendered A-tab palette rather than from a sheet — the A tabs compose one cell per kind, so sheet addressing drew nothing. A cut-out's own picture cannot change as the view turns, which is why the ground had to carry the rotation.

- `drawTile3DPreview` no longer foreshortens or column-reverses as it turns. A yaw-billboard presents the same picture from every azimuth, so only the ground plane and the flat-role pieces rotate; treating the object as solid mirrored its columns and produced a broken silhouette from behind.

- `Tileset3DClass.defineObject` rejects tile 0, which is the engine's "no tile". `objectAt` already refused it, so a declaration anchored there could be created and never found again.

- Key rows are canvases drawn by `drawKeyMark`, which reuses `drawFlagDot`/`drawFlagArrow`/`drawFlagRect`/`drawBushMark` — the same helpers the tile overlay calls. The first version used tinted `<span>`s and came out blank for Ladder, Bush and Counter, whose overlays are glyphs rather than tints, and it could have drifted from the overlay without anything noticing. `drawBushMark` replaces the bush's `drawFlagDot`, since a green dot was indistinguishable from the direction dots.

- The 3D class marker draws only on a declared object's top-left cell (`markHere`); the tint still covers every cell. Drawn per cell, a 2x2 mountain read as four separate mountains — exactly what the declaration exists to deny.

- `DatabaseTilesetEditor.flagKey` renders a per-mode key into `#flag-mode-key`, and `refreshTile3DPreview` draws the selected tile or declared object into `#tile3d-preview` — both in the margins the centred sheet was already leaving empty, so nothing the author was looking at moves. The preview is an oblique diagram drawn on a 2D canvas from the tile's own art rather than a real render: showing one tile through the actual renderer means loading three.js, the sheets and a scene, and what needs checking is which parts stand and which lie flat. Both panels are English for now — routing them adds nineteen phrases across seventeen locales and the wording is still settling. Coverage in `tileset-3d-authoring.test.cjs`.

- `DatabaseTilesetEditor.flagIcon` draws an inline SVG for each flag mode, replacing the ASCII prefixes that only three of the eight buttons had. One 24-unit grid, `currentColor` so a selected button's icon follows its label, and each drawn as a few strokes because these render at 16px where anything finer smudges — checked by rasterising at 16 and 96. `tileset-3d-authoring.test.cjs` asserts every mode has one and that each button renders it.

- Cut-outs are grouped into whole objects. `Geometry.uprightObjects` replaces the per-column runs: cells join an object when they are adjacent on the map *and* adjacent the same way on the sheet, which is what makes a stamped picture a picture. Plain 4-connectivity was tried first and merged 408 cells of unrelated props across 44x27 tiles into one anchor on the world map; each object turns about a single axis through the middle of its southern row, so a wide structure holds together instead of fanning into one card per column. The anchor is what the object's position reads as, and both other candidates were visibly wrong: on the southern *edge* a five-wide structure sat over its cells from the south and a tile clear of them from the east, and on the footprint centre its base hung above the ground in front of it at a low camera. Autotile uprights are excluded and keep the fixed-plane path — a wall belongs to a building and has a real facing, and swinging it to the camera would rotate the building.

- B-E props derive as `UPRIGHT` when `standsUp` holds — impassable on all four sides, or starred — instead of requiring the star flag alone. On Map596 that flag test left tile 28 (a lone tree, 147 cells), 40 and 48 (mountains), 112 and 122 (landed ships) and 92 (scrub) lying flat while the canopy beside them stood: ★ is set only where a character walks *behind* something, so it is a lower bound on height and misses every object a character walks in front of. The test runs per block of art via `sheetBlocks`, so one qualifying piece raises the whole picture — the Infernis gantry is three rows tall and only its top row is starred, and per tile that stood its head up and left its legs on the floor. Pits and craters are the known false positive: the art blocks the way like an object and is a hole in the ground, so it wants `Flat` set by hand, once per tileset.

- A fourth 3D class, `CLASS_FOLIAGE`, for terrain that is a repeated object rather than a mass. It stands cut-outs on the cell taken from the terrain's lone variant — `classKey(id) + 46` for an autotile, since shape 46 is what MZ draws for a single isolated cell, and a recorded `standIns` entry for a B-E tile, which the generator guesses as the same-sized block directly above the tiling art on the sheet. Stand-ins are stored as `[topLeftTile, width, height]`: a lone variant is usually drawn over a block, and sampling only its first tile stood up the top-left quarter of each tree. `foliageDensity` cut-outs per cell (1 by default — a cell is one instance of the terrain, so it gets one object; several per cell multiplied the geometry to break up a grid the position hash already breaks up), each scaled 0.75-1.25 and displaced by up to `foliageSpread` from the centre through a position hash, so a wood is scattered but rebuilds identically; `foliageLift` raises the cell 0.3 so the wood has a visible edge; and `foliageFloor` is off, so the tiling art is not laid flat as well — it is the same terrain seen from above, so drawing both draws each cell twice and shows as a mat of canopy at the feet of the trees. Two alternatives were built and rejected against renders: keeping the floor art matched 2D from overhead and produced the mat, and capping the interior of a mass with the tiling art while standing cut-outs only on its border — `Geometry`'s `foliageBorder`, kept — was ideal overhead and made forests into flat terraces with a tree fringe from the ground. The 3D Shape editor cycles Flat → Upright → Scenery → Foliage → Auto. Coverage in `reactor-3d-geometry.test.cjs` and `tileset-3d-class.test.cjs`.

### Fixed

- Billboard cut-outs rendering dark and unfogged. `Reactor3D.billboardMaterial` was a `ShaderMaterial` whose fragment shader wrote the texel straight to `gl_FragColor`, skipping three.js's `colorspace_fragment` conversion from the texture's colour space to the renderer's output — so a tree was visibly darker than the same art lying flat beside it — and skipping the fog chunks, so the editor's `THREE.Fog` never reached it. It is a `MeshBasicMaterial` with an `onBeforeCompile` that replaces `#include <begin_vertex>`, keeping every other chunk the flat tiles use; `customProgramCacheKey` stops three.js compiling a program per material, since it cannot see the injected code.

- Vertical faces in 3D sampling the wrong layer. `Geometry.build` took a cliff face's art from `stack[stack.length - 1]`, the topmost layer of the cell, and above a step that is normally a decoration whose art is transparent everywhere the decoration is not — so the face alpha-tested away to nothing and a raised region stood on a rim of holes. Faces take `stack[0]` now, which is the terrain.

- 3D tile classification derived from the tileset flags standing terrain on end. `Reactor3D.uprightPredicate` treats an explicit `CLASS_UPRIGHT` as authored and `uprightRuns` therefore exempts it from `AUTO_MAX_FACADE` — deliberately, since a tileset may draw a tower fifty tiles tall as one prop — so a `Tilesets.r3d.json` filled in from `flag & 0x10 || (flag & 0x0f) === 0x0f` had no ceiling at all. On Map596 (200x200) that produced 685 facades, 64 of them past the cap and the tallest sixteen tiles, and connected-region analysis put 1,587 of the 2,337 standing cells inside regions of 4x4 or larger — terrain, not props. `build-scripts/derive-tileset-3d-classes.cjs` derives the file from map usage instead. Each B-E placement is judged in a 5x5 neighbourhood: covered ground whose tiles repeat is terrain (`SCENERY`), sparse or non-repeating company is a prop, and a tile reading as terrain in a quarter of its placements is terrain everywhere, since a misjudged prop rises a tile while a misjudged forest becomes a wall. The neighbourhood replaced a connected-region test that let a crater painted at a forest's edge inherit the forest's verdict and rise on a plinth. `0x10` alone decides height for props — impassable covers craters and rubble too, and honouring it put the map on stilts — and autotiles are read off the sheet: A1 flat whatever its flags, A3 and the A4 wall rows upright, blocked A2 and A4 roof rows raised as a mass. Only painted tiles are written. Coverage in `tileset-3d-derivation.test.cjs`.

- A tile selection surviving a palette layer switch. `selectLayer` reassigned `currentLayer` and re-rendered, but never touched `selectedTiles`, so the entries — sheet coordinates tagged with the layer they were taken from — stayed live. The redrawn palette had nothing to highlight, since the selection belonged to a sheet no longer on screen, while a click still resolved those entries against their original layer and painted its tiles. The selection is now cleared when the layer actually changes, the readout is reset to "No tiles selected" to match, and any hover preview built from it is dropped, since nothing rebuilds one until the pointer moves again. Re-asserting the current layer leaves the selection alone, and the clear is guarded on the outgoing value, so it has to run before `currentLayer` is reassigned or every switch would read as a no-op. Coverage in `palette-layer-switch.test.cjs`.

- Tileset sheets F and G erasing instead of painting. `MapEditor` carries two routines that turn a palette position into a tile id — `getTileIdFromPalettePosition` and `getBaseTileIdFromPalettePosition` — and only the first was extended when F/G landed. The second fell through its `switch` default and returned `0`, which is the empty tile, so committing a placement wiped the cell. Everything that merely *displays* a tile resolves its texture separately, which is why the palette and the hover preview both looked correct and only painting failed. Both routines now share one case driven by `RRTilesetSheets.baseTileIdForSheet`, and `tileset-extended-sheets.test.cjs` asserts every sheet key is handled in both, so a future sheet cannot be added to one and forgotten in the other.

- Newly created database records missing fields the runtime reads unconditionally. `getDefaultTemplates` was diffed against the field sets of every record in the bundled RPG Maker-authored projects, and four types were short. **Skills** lacked `requiredWtypeId1`/`requiredWtypeId2`, and `Game_Actor.isSkillWtypeOk` opens with `wtypeId1 === 0 && wtypeId2 === 0` — `undefined === 0` is false and `undefined > 0` is false, so every clause failed and a skill created in Reactor could never be used by any actor. **Weapons** lacked `etypeId`, which `changeEquip` compares against `equipSlots()[slotId]`, so a new weapon never appeared in the equip list and equipping it silently did nothing. **Items** lacked `tpGain`, and `applyItemUserEffect` computes `Math.floor(item.tpGain * tcr)`, so using one set the user's TP to `NaN` for the rest of the battle. **States** lacked `motion` and `overlay`, which `stateMotionIndex`/`stateOverlayIndex` return directly to the battler sprites, so a new state produced no pose and no overlay. The skill template also invented `message3`/`message4`, which no authored skill carries, and omitted `messageType`. This reached existing projects too: `createBlankDatabaseEntry` backs both the Clear action and the repair pass for null slots. The derived field and command shapes are vendored in `tests/helpers/authored-data-shapes.json` (regenerate with `helpers/derive-authored-data-shapes.cjs`) so the check runs in CI without the private project fixtures. Coverage in `database-record-templates.test.cjs`.

- Scroll Map dropping Wait for Completion: `command204` reads `params[3]` to decide `setWaitMode("scroll")`, but `ScrollMapEditor` had no control for it, read only `params[0..2]` when opening a command, and emitted a three-element array. The option could therefore never be turned on, and opening an imported command that had it set and pressing OK silently discarded the flag. The editor now carries `wait` through load and save and offers the same checkbox the Tint Screen, Flash Screen, and Shake Screen editors use. A sweep comparing every command editor's emitted parameter count against the highest `params[n]` the matching `Game_Interpreter.commandNNN` reads found this to be the only editor writing an array the interpreter reads past the end of; that sweep now runs as a test. Coverage in `event-command-parameters.test.cjs`.

- The window title showing the bundled demo's name for every project. `installCompatibilityTitlebar` built its label from the literal string `RPG Reactor | Reactor One` and nothing ever wrote to it again, so the title was frozen at the demo project's name no matter what was open. `updateWindowTitle` composes the correct `app.title | gameTitle` and sets `document.title` and `nw.Window.get().title`, but the compatibility titlebar is a `<div>` — neither of those reaches it. The titlebar is now seeded from the live title at construction and rewritten by `updateWindowTitle`, so it follows project open, close, save and language change like the native one. It reproduces only under frameless compatibility mode (`?rrFrameless=1` / `?rrWineFrame=0`), which is the self-relaunch used for Wine/Proton — a normal framed window keeps the native titlebar and was never affected, which is why this showed up for packaged users and not in development. Coverage in `window-title.test.cjs`.

- Set Event Location's "Exchange with another event" reading and writing the wrong slots. `command203` uses `params[2]` as the **other character's id** — there is no map id in this command form — and `params[4]` as the direction. The editor parsed `params[2]` as a map id and invented an event id from `params[3] || 1`, labelled them "Map ID" and "Event ID", and emitted only four parameters, so the direction slot was truncated away on every save. Both authored instances in the bundled projects are `[charId, 2, otherCharId, 0, direction]`; the editor now loads and rebuilds them byte-identically, and offers a direction control in place of the invented map id.

- A1 waterfall kinds 9 and 11 drawn from the wrong table in map-image export. `drawAutotileToCanvas` gated the waterfall table on `Math.floor(tx / 4) === 1`, which is false for exactly those two kinds — they fell through to the floor table and sampled a different 96px band of the sheet. The engine (`reactor_core.js`) and all three sibling render paths select it unconditionally, and `renderAutotile` in the same file carries a comment saying so. Kind 9 appears **2,149 times across 21 maps** in the bundled projects, kind 11 **74 times across 5**. Affected Save Map as Image and the right-drag map-stamp preview.

- The eraser swallowing the bucket fill. The pointerdown dispatch let `eraserMode` claim the click ahead of the tool branches, so selecting eraser + bucket erased a single cell like the pencil. `eraseFillArea` — 37 lines, fully implemented and correct — was unreachable from the UI. The eraser is now treated as a modifier on the active tool rather than a tool of its own, matching how the rectangle and circle tools already handled it.

- The web editor had no keyboard shortcuts at all. `setupKeyboardShortcuts` opened with `if (typeof nw === 'undefined') return;`, but only the F5, F11 and F12 branches need NW.js — the early return also removed Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Z/Y, Ctrl+C/X/V and Delete. The NW-specific branches now guard individually, matching the `typeof nw === 'undefined' && !window.RPGReactorHost` pattern used elsewhere, and `reloadApplicationIgnoringCache` degrades to `location.reload()`.

- The loop toggle not reaching the playing audio. `toggleLoop` flipped `audioPlayer.loop` and restyled the button, but the flag only reached a media element when a track started, so turning loop off mid-playback did nothing audible. It now sets `loop` on the current channel as well.

- Sidebar divider positions saved and then always discarded. `loadSavedSizes` runs once from `initialize()` — before any project is open — and skips `display: none` sections, which every id-bearing section is at that point. `refresh()`, which runs when sections become visible, never re-applied them. It now does, after `initializeSectionFlexValues` so the restored values win.

- Imported character sheets getting a different cell size per frame. The 3x4 crop loop rounded every cell boundary independently, so any sheet whose dimensions are not divisible by 3x4 produced adjacent cells differing by a pixel — and `RR_CG_drawTemplatePart` right-pads short rows rather than centring them, so the character shifted horizontally on the middle walk frame. The origin is still rounded per cell but the width and height are now shared, which makes every checked import uniform. Where the source image genuinely runs out of pixels on the last row, the normalizer below repairs the residue.

- "Normalize All Templates" reporting success while leaving frame heights unequal. `_padSheetInPlace` computed one sheet-wide `padTop`/`padBottom`, so a frame with fewer rows than the sheet maximum stayed short of the target while widths — which are padded per row — came out correct. Padding is now derived per frame. The 279 shipped part descriptors were already uniform; this only ever bit the user-import path.

- Map window culling parking windows that were still on screen. `_rrWindowDormant` tested only `child.x`/`child.y`, but `Window.prototype.move` assigns those as the **top-left corner** with `width`/`height` extending right and down — so a window wider than the 384px margin still overlapped the viewport while its corner sat outside it. Culled windows are *detached from the display tree*, not merely hidden, so the result is disappearance rather than a paint glitch. The character-sprite branch of the same method, sixty lines above, already carried exactly this correction with a comment explaining it; the window branch never got it. The predicate now culls on the drawn extent. Its opacity test was widened too: `contentsOpacity` only reaches `_contentsSprite.alpha`, while `_contentsBackSprite` and everything added through `addInnerChild` (`Sprite_Gauge`, `Sprite_Name`, `Sprite_StateIcon`) are independent siblings in the client area with their own alpha, so a transparent-framed window drawing through those was judged to contribute nothing and parked. Coverage in `window-culling.test.cjs`, which lifts the shipped predicate rather than restating it.

- A window stepped twice on the frame it was parked. `Spriteset_Base.update` cascades `update()` to every child before `updateOffscreenCulling` runs, and the catch-up loop then re-reached the window the first loop had just appended to the holder. This is one extra tick per park transition, not a sustained rate — a doubled openness step during a fade, and a repeat of any side effect a plugin's `update()` performs. The transition is now recorded and consumed once.

- **Party-wide actor commands collapsed onto Actor 0001.** `Game_Interpreter.iterateActorId` branches on `param === 0` to mean *the entire party*, and nine command editors loaded that slot as `params[1] || 1` — 0 is falsy, so every party-wide command was rewritten to the first actor the moment it was opened, and the edit path writes the loaded value straight back. The actor dropdowns also started at index 1, so the value could not be expressed at all. In the bundled projects this covers **284 of 284** Change HP commands, **376 of 379** Recover All, 94 of 114 Change State and 3 of 7 Change Skill: open the inn's "Recover All → Entire Party" event, press OK, and from then on only Actor 0001 is healed. Fixed across `ChangeHPEditor`, `ChangeMPEditor`, `ChangeTPEditor`, `ChangeEXPEditor`, `ChangeLevelEditor`, `ChangeParameterEditor`, `ChangeStateEditor`, `ChangeSkillEditor` and `RecoverAllEditor`, each of which now loads with `??` and offers an **Entire Party** option translated into all 17 non-English locales.

- Show Text losing the Top window position: `parseCommand` read `command.parameters[3] || 2`, and position `0` is Top. **946 of the 18,520** authored Show Text commands in the bundled projects use it. Opening one and pressing OK moved the message window to the bottom, and the dropdown showed the wrong value while open. Every sibling in the family already guarded this correctly — `ShowChoicesCommandEditor` uses `params[3] !== undefined ? … : 2`, and the adjacent `background` read uses `|| 0` because 0 *is* its default — so this was a single outlier.

- Fadeout BGM/BGS authored in frames while the engine reads seconds. `AudioManager.fadeOutBgm(params[0])` reaches `WebAudio._fadeOut`, which calls `linearRampToValueAtTime(0, currentTime + duration)` — a Web Audio API that takes **seconds**. `AudioCommandEditor` defaulted to `[60]` "= 1 second", divided by 60 on load and multiplied by 60 on save. Inserting the command with its default therefore produced a **60-second** fade, and a 5-second setting produced a five-minute one; conversely an imported MZ command reading `5` displayed as `0.083 s`. Every authored 242/246 in the corpus holds a small seconds count. The conversion is gone from the editor and from the command-list label, which had repeated the same assumption.

- Event graphics taken from the right half of a B–E tileset page saved the wrong tile. `TilesetPaletteViewer` presents those sheets as a 16-wide split and reports `x` in 0..15; `MapEditor.getBaseTileIdFromPalettePosition` folds `x >= 8` into `y += 16`, but `EventManager.convertToTileId` did not. Palette B (8,0) became tile 8 instead of 128 — a different tile entirely, written into `Map###.json` and shown wrongly in both the editor and the game. Right-half tiles are ordinary authoring territory: **132,054** placements against 214,715 left-half across the corpus. Verified by feeding both conversions through the engine's own `sx/sy` frame math.

- Plugin command metadata overwriting plugin parameter defaults. `parsePluginParameters` walked the annotation block without resetting on `@command`/`@arg`, so every `@default` belonging to a plugin **command argument** landed on the last real `@param`. `parsePluginParameterMetadata` has carried that guard all along, making this a two-parser divergence. Adding an affected plugin through the Plugin Manager wrote the wrong default to `js/plugins.js` — `debugMode: "1"` where `false` was declared (neither value the runtime's boolean check accepts), `"Gold Icon Index": "true"` where `163` was declared. Both parsers now agree on every bundled plugin, which the test checks by lifting them out of the source and running them over the real plugin set.

- Six of the eight System 1 → Music rows read and wrote keys the engine never looks at. The table synthesised `` `${type}Bgm` `` for all eight, but System.json only spells Title and Battle that way: Victory, Defeat and Game Over are **ME** entries (`victoryMe`, `defeatMe`, and `gameoverMe` — lowercase), and vehicle music nests as `boat.bgm` / `ship.bgm` / `airship.bgm`. `template/Complex` has a real `gameoverMe` of `Seka - WhatIsLeft` that the editor rendered as "(None)", and picking a file for any of the six had no in-game effect while injecting a junk key into System.json that then travelled into every `Test_System.json`. A `resolveMusicSlot` helper now maps each row to the slot the engine reads, for both display and save.

- A newly created animation left `displayType` unset. `Spriteset_Base.isAnimationForEach` tests `animation.displayType === 0`, so an absent value is false and the animation plays **once at the centroid of the target group** instead of once per target — while the editor's own dropdown, which marks no option selected, displays the first entry and claims per-target. All **648** MZ-format authored animations carry the field. The record-template guard now separates MZ-format animations from MV-format ones, which had been diluting the required field set down to just `name`.

- The Traits and Effects tables in the Skill, Item, Weapon, Armor and State editors never refreshed after an edit. All five called `document.querySelector('.database-detail-panel')` — a class that exists in no HTML or CSS in the repository; the pane is `#database-detail`. The selector returned `null` and the redraw silently did nothing, across all six call sites per file (add / edit / cut / copy / paste / delete). The Enemy editor is the one sibling that was ever repaired, and it still carries the stale class as a fallback. Beyond the stale display this made **Delete destructive past the selection**: the index comes from the un-redrawn DOM, so a second Delete removes a row the user never selected, and trait edits have no undo.

- Event page conditions written with the wrong type. `attachConditionListeners` decided how to store a value from the *element* type — `if (e.target.type === 'number') value = parseInt(value)` — which describes the control, not the field. Two consequences. A `<select>` reports type `select-one`, so **Actor** and **Item** conditions were stored as strings (`"3"`), where all 49,371 authored pages in the bundled projects hold numbers; the stock runtime tolerates this because JS object keys are strings, but any plugin comparing `page.conditions.actorId` strictly sees a mismatch, and the project no longer round-trips. More seriously, **clearing the variable-threshold field** stored `parseInt('')`, i.e. `NaN`, which `JSON.stringify` writes as `null`; the runtime's check is a bare `$gameVariables.value(c.variableId) < c.variableValue`, so `< null` is `< 0` and the page began meeting a condition it never had before. Conversion is now driven by the field: the six numeric conditions are parsed as integers, `selfSwitchCh` stays a string, and an empty or unparseable entry keeps the previous value instead of writing something the engine cannot compare. Zero and negative thresholds are preserved, since both are legitimate. Coverage in `event-page-conditions.test.cjs`, including a check that every field the form exposes and authored data holds as a number is on the numeric list.

- Two editor sources referenced by `index.html` were never committed: `src/EditorInstanceBroker.js` and `src/event/EventCollapsePreferences.js`, both added earlier in this cycle. They loaded on the machine that wrote them and would have 404'd for everyone else. Both are now tracked. `shipped-file-references.test.cjs` resolves every local `src`/`href` in `editor/index.html` and `template/Demo/index.html` and asserts each target both exists and is committed, so a forgotten `git add` on a new source file fails the suite instead of shipping. The scan also asserts it matched a plausible number of scripts, since a regex that quietly matched nothing would make the check vacuous.

- The bundled Demo project could not boot from a clean checkout. `template/Demo/js/reactor_main.js` lists `js/libs/lz-string.js` and `js/reactor_picture_extensions.js` in `scriptUrls`, and both files existed on disk but had never been committed — `.gitignore` re-includes `/template/Demo/**`, so nothing was excluding them; they were simply never added. `template/Demo` is the one project directory the repository tracks and `dist-editor-worker.js` bundles it into the editor, so a fresh clone or a CI build shipped a Demo missing two of its sixteen boot scripts. The failure is total rather than degraded: `onScriptLoad` only advances `loadCount` on success and `onScriptError` merely reports, so `onScriptsLoaded` never fires and the Demo stops on the loading spinner. Both files are now tracked and are byte-identical to their `runtime/` originals. `demo-template-completeness.test.cjs` parses the boot manifest, asserts every entry is present *and* tracked in git, checks the Effekseer wasm the same way, and compares each shipped runtime file against `runtime/` so the templates cannot drift again unnoticed. The private-fixture guard in `release-infrastructure.test.cjs` was narrowed to permit `template/Demo` specifically — it is tracked and available in CI — while still blocking traversal of `template` itself.

- Terrain tags silently not applying on tiles whose flag exceeds 16 bits. `Game_Map.terrainTag` is `flags[tile] >> 12` with no mask, but the tileset editor cleared only bits 12-15 when writing a tag (`oldFlag & ~0xF000`) and masked to `0x0F` when displaying one. On a flag with bits above 15 set the two disagree: setting terrain 3 on `0x02CBDE0F` produced `0x02CB3E0F`, which the editor showed as 3 and the engine read as **11443**. The control appeared to work and changed nothing the game could see. The write now clears everything from bit 12 up (`oldFlag & 0x0FFF`), which preserves every flag the engine defines — passage `0x0f`, star `0x10`, ladder `0x20`, bush `0x40`, counter `0x80`, damage floor `0x100` all live below bit 9 — and the two palette renderers read the tag with the same unmasked shift the engine uses, so a flag the game reads as garbage is no longer displayed as a plausible 0-15. Surfaced by decoding real project data: `Star Shift Rebellion` carries 1,200 such flags in each of five tilesets, written by `Cyclone-Map-Editor-MV`. Coverage in `tileset-flag-semantics.test.cjs`, which lifts the shipped cycle out of the editor and checks it against the engine's own bit reads.

- Web editor asset routing mis-resolving nested paths: the service worker located the project root by taking the **last** `/project/` in the request path, so any asset stored under a folder the game itself names `project` matched the wrong occurrence — `project/img/pictures/project/logo.png` resolved to `logo.png` and either served an unrelated file or fell through to a 404. The routing now anchors on `self.registration.scope`, which is where `WebHost` mounts its `/project` virtual root, and requires the path to start there. That also confines interception to the worker's own origin and scope, where before any URL containing the marker was intercepted. Coverage in `web-service-worker-routing.test.cjs`, which loads the shipped worker against a stub `self` rather than reimplementing the rule.

- Battle Test database copies shipping in deployed games: `BattleTestConfigModal` writes a `Test_`-prefixed copy of all fourteen database files into `data/` and never removes them — they are regenerated on the next battle test and are only read in `btest` mode. Neither `build.js` nor `build-worker.js` excluded them, so every release carried a full duplicate of the database: **15 MB in `template/Complex`, 13 MB in `Star Shift Rebellion`**, plus `Test_System.json` exposing the developer's saved test party. Both staging paths now share an `isStagingExcluded` predicate that drops `data/Test_*.json` alongside the existing `Backup`/`save`/`Screenshots` rules. The rule is anchored to `data/` and to `.json`, so a plugin or asset legitimately named `Test_…` is unaffected. Coverage in `deployment-staging-exclusions.test.cjs`, which lifts the shipped predicate out of both scripts rather than reimplementing it, and pins the two copies against drift.

- Non-atomic writes to two critical project files. `FsAtomic.js` exists because a truncate-in-place `writeFileSync` destroys the previous good file along with the new one if the process dies mid-write, and its header names `System.json` and the database JSON specifically — but two call sites still bypassed it. `DatabaseTilesetEditor.saveTilesets` wrote `data/Tilesets.json` directly; it is the largest database file, since each tileset carries an 8192-entry flags array, so it has the widest exposure window of any of them. `BattleTestConfigModal.launch` wrote `data/System.json` and the `Test_` copies directly; `System.json` holds terms, starting party, vehicles, and the boot map, so a partial write leaves the project unopenable. Both now use the same `_writeFileAtomic` wrapper as `DatabaseManager`, `ProjectController`, `ProjectManager`, `PluginManager`, and `TilemapManager`, degrading to a plain write only where `renameSync` is unavailable (test mocks, the web host shim). A sweep now walks every `writeFileSync` in `editor/src`, resolves the target path through nearby assignments, and fails on any that names a critical project file without going through the wrapper. Coverage in `atomic-project-writes.test.cjs`.

- Plugin commands losing their display label on re-edit: `params[2]` of a `357` holds the command's human-readable name — the string from the plugin's `@text` annotation, which is what the event list shows. `PluginCommandEditor` never read it and wrote a hard-coded `''` with the comment "params[2] is unused in MZ", so opening any authored plugin command and pressing OK erased the label in the saved project. In the corpus every authored `357` carries one (`['PSYCHRONIC_PTBS', 'PTBS_StartBattle', 'Start PTBS Battle']`). The editor already parsed `@text` into the command dropdown, so the label is now taken from the annotation when the selected command supplies one and from the loaded command otherwise — the latter covering plugins whose source file is missing or unannotated. Switching plugin or command clears the stale value rather than carrying it across. The event list prefers the label over the internal command name, falling back to it for commands written before this change and for MV's `356`, which has no such slot. Found by sweeping every command editor for parameter slots it emits but never loads, which is the same shape as the Scroll Map wait flag. Coverage in `plugin-command-label.test.cjs`.

- `When [Choice]` branch markers written without the choice text: `ShowChoicesCommandEditor` emitted `parameters: [index]`, while all 2,570 authored `402` commands in the bundled projects carry `[index, text]` — as do Reactor's own Common Event and Troop page templates, making the choices editor the outlier. Only the index drives branching, so gameplay was unaffected, but the command list had nothing to label the branch with and fell back to a bare `Choice 1`, and the project no longer round-tripped through tooling that reads the name. The marker now stores the text and the list renders it, keeping the ordinal as a fallback for markers written before this change.

- Unescaped project-authored text in the event header: `EventEditor` interpolated `${event.name}` into a `value="…"` attribute and `${event.note}` into a `<textarea>` body inside `innerHTML` templates, with no escaping. A quote truncated the attribute and a `</textarea>` closed the element early, so the mangled value was read back from the DOM and saved — degrading the project data itself — and in an NW.js window with Node enabled, markup injected from an imported project executes with full filesystem and process access. Both now use `rrEscapeHtml`. This was the class 0.95.0 swept for project names, notes, and messages; the event header was missed. A new sweep parses every template literal assigned to `innerHTML` across `editor/src`, extracts interpolations reading user-authored record fields, and fails on any that do not pass through an escape helper — the two remaining hits are developer-authored schema text and are allowlisted explicitly. Coverage in `markup-escaping.test.cjs`.

- Audio commands inserted at indent 0 inside a branch body: every event command editor builds its command at `indent: 0` and relies on the insertion path to rebase it onto `EventCommandList.insertionIndent(list, insertIndex)`. The audio branch (codes 241, 242, 245, 246, 249, 250, 251) spliced `editedCommand` directly with no rebase — the single insertion site of 102 that neither rebased nor set `indent: baseIndent` inline. The consequence is not cosmetic: `Game_Interpreter.skipBranch` is `while (this._list[this._index + 1].indent > this._indent) this._index++`, so a body command left at the branch's own indent halts the skip, and it plus every later command in that body execute even when the condition is false. The edit path was already safe because `AudioCommandEditor` clones the original command and preserves its indent; only newly inserted commands were affected. Coverage in `event-insert-indent.test.cjs`, including a check that no insertion site establishes a command without setting its indent.

- Animation timing SE volume discarding a deliberate zero: the save handler read `parseInt(...) || 90`, and `0` is falsy, so a timing dragged to silence on a `min="0"` slider was written back at volume 90. Replaced with `DatabaseAnimationEditor.readNumericInput`, which keeps any finite value — including 0 and negatives — and falls back only when the field is missing, empty, or unparseable. A sweep of every `min="0"` control in the editor and Forge against truthy `||` defaults found this to be the only instance; the neighbouring `timing-duration` (`min="1"`) and `timing-se-pitch` (`min="50"`) reads cannot reach 0 and were left unchanged. Coverage in `animation-timing-se-volume.test.cjs`.

- Silent IndexedDB write failures in the web host: `createFileSystem` queued each `put`/`delete` promise into `pending` and attached only `.finally(() => pending.delete(operation))`. `finally` passes a rejection through rather than handling it, so a failed write produced an unhandled rejection **and** removed itself from `pending` before `flush()` awaited the set — `flush()` then resolved successfully with nothing written, while the in-memory `contents` map still held the new data. The project appeared saved for the rest of the session and reverted to its last persisted state on reload; browser quota exhaustion is the realistic trigger. Failures are now recorded per path, `flush()` uses `Promise.allSettled` so one failure cannot discard the writes that succeeded, and it throws an error carrying the failed paths. The report drains, so a later flush does not re-raise a failure already surfaced. `hasPendingWriteFailures()` exposes the state for callers that want to check without flushing. Coverage in `web-storage-write-failures.test.cjs`.

- Undo restoring a wrong-length map array after a resize: `MapEditor.undo` assigned `this.tilemapManager.currentMap.data = previousData` with no size check, and `saveMapProperties` cleared no history. A paint followed by a Map Properties resize therefore left snapshots sized `oldWidth * oldHeight * 6` on the stack while `currentMap.width/height` described the new size; undoing produced a `data` array that disagreed with its own dimensions, which renders as garbage because every index is computed as `layer * (w * h) + y * w + x`, and persists to `Map###.json` on the next save. `applyMapResize` now clears both the paint history and `EventManager`'s (a resize moves events and deletes those the new bounds exclude, so an event undo would restore them at coordinates the map no longer has), and `undo`/`redo` call `dropStaleUndoStates` first so any snapshot whose length no longer matches `width * height * 6` is discarded rather than applied — closing the same hole for any other path that resizes a map. Coverage in `map-undo-resize-safety.test.cjs`.
- Non-atomic shared clipboard write: `ReactorClipboard.writeSharedFile` used a truncate-in-place `writeFileSync` on the file that carries typed payloads between editor instances. A whole-map envelope is large enough to be read mid-write by the receiving instance, which sees a partial document and fails the paste; a crash mid-write leaves the file corrupt until the next successful copy. It now routes through the same `RRWriteFileAtomicSync` rename-over helper as project data, guarded on `renameSync` existing so mocks and the web host still work.

- Sliver tiles misread as transparent: `TilesetPaletteViewer.isSelectionTransparent` sampled `getImageData(12, 12, 24, 24)` — the centre 24x24 of a 48x48 tile — leaving a 12px border unexamined. A B-E or A5 tile whose art is only a few rows at one edge, the continuation of an object in the neighbouring tile, therefore read as fully transparent, and `autoToggleEraseMode` armed the eraser: painting with it erased instead of placing. Selecting it alongside the tile above short-circuited the check on the first opaque tile, which is why the pair worked and the sliver alone did not. The full tile is now sampled; the source rect is drawn 1:1 into a cleared scratch canvas, so there is no neighbouring-tile bleed the crop was guarding against. Genuinely empty tiles still report transparent and still arm the eraser, and autotile layers (A1-A4) remain excluded because their palette grid is a rendered preview rather than sheet cells. Coverage in `tileset-transparency-detection.test.cjs`.

- Collapsed Tileset dropdown on an unresolvable `tilesetId`: `populateMapPropertiesForm` assigned `select.value = mapData.tilesetId || 1` directly, and `populateTilesetDropdown` emits options only for non-null tilesets. Assigning a value that matches no option leaves `selectedIndex` at `-1`, so the control draws no text and shrinks to its padding — the reported "crunched" dropdown, which recovered as soon as any real entry was chosen. Reached by a map whose tileset was cleared from the database, or by a new map defaulting to id 1 in a project whose tilesets begin at a higher id. `selectTilesetOption` now falls back to the first available option when the requested id does not match, `populateTilesetDropdown` appends a `(None)` placeholder when a project has no tilesets at all so the element is never optionless, and the select carries a `min-height` so it cannot collapse visually even with nothing selected. Coverage in `map-tileset-dropdown.test.cjs`.

- Always-visible Battleback picker: `#map-battleback-picker` declared `display` twice in one style attribute (`display: none; … display: grid;`), and the later declaration wins — so the selectors rendered regardless of the Specify Battleback checkbox. The duplicate is removed, and both toggle sites now set `grid` rather than `flex`, which had discarded the element's own `grid-template-columns`. Covered by a test that rejects a second `display` declaration on any collapsible picker.
- Web editor application menu: the word-based File/Database/Plugins/Tools/Forge/Help bar no longer clips its absolutely positioned dropdowns below a web-only `overflow-y: hidden` boundary. The bar uses visible overflow and wraps at narrow widths; the independent icon toolbar remains horizontally scrollable. Source and generated-web-package tests enforce the non-clipping rule.
- Map tree New Map placement: the right-click target now flows through the map-properties modal and anchors insertion. Confirmed maps are placed immediately after that map in its normalized sibling group, preserving child hierarchy; calls without an explicit context target fall back to the currently highlighted map, and invalid anchors still append safely to the root group.
- Auto-layer bucket preservation: flood filling an A-layer floor now writes only its resolved destination plane, leaving existing upper B-E and A-wall content intact. The post-fill autotile pass reconnects only planes actually changed by the operation, preventing a lower fill from silently changing upper wall shape IDs. Decoration fills still replace their resolved second A slot, while explicit-layer and topmost eraser semantics are unchanged.
- Packaged multi-instance startup: modern NW.js ignores the deprecated `single-instance: false` manifest field, so repeated native launches were redirected into the first process. The editor now brokers subsequent Windows, macOS, and Linux launches into detached processes with distinct atomically leased `--user-data-dir` profile slots. Live slots are never shared, stale leases recover conservatively, slot preferences persist, and project-level locking remains independent.
- Windows developer package finalization: the worker now resolves and requires `resedit` only inside the `releaseBuild` metadata branch. Interactive normal/SDK packages skip PE metadata editing and no longer fail after downloading and extracting NW.js; release candidates still require and verify app-owned Windows metadata before signing.
- Top-level database Clear/Delete consistency: toolbar Delete, keyboard Delete/Backspace, and context-menu Cut now share one blank-template operation. Clearing Tilesets 1-5 and 11-15 leaves all 15 array slots and embedded IDs intact, renders cleared slots as `Unnamed #N`, and survives JSON save/reload without hidden `null` gaps or tail shrinkage. The prior toolbar-only `null` behavior explained the apparent packaged-versus-development discrepancy.
- Null-slot repair scope: `DatabaseManager.deleteEntry` nulls the addressed slot for any `dataKey`, so pre-0.96.0 toolbar deletion could persist hidden gaps in every top-level list, not only Tilesets. `restoreNullDatabaseEntries` now runs once for the opened type ahead of the `openDatabase` switch, repairing Actors, Classes, Skills, Items, Weapons, Armors, Enemies, Troops, States, Animations, Common Events, and Tilesets alike. Index 0 stays reserved, populated records are untouched, and types with no backing array or template no-op.
- Locale completeness: `RR_ADDITIONAL_LOCALES` merges its overrides over `RR_I18N_STRINGS.en`, so `zh-Hant`, `zh-Hans`, `ru`, `pt`, `de`, `fr`, and `el` silently rendered every key added after those tables were written. Between 118 and 144 of 392 keys per locale resolved to English, concentrated in the Effekseer Forge, event-page fields, and toolbar tooltips. All seven tables are completed, and genuine gaps in `es`, `it`, `id`, `vi`, and `tr` are filled. Key-set parity tests could not catch this because the inherited keys do exist; two new tests assert instead that no locale resolves to the English string outside a documented proper-noun and loanword allowlist, and that the allowlist itself contains no entries that have since been translated.
- Instance slot takeover race: `tryReserveSlot` judged a lock stale, deleted it, and re-created it as three unsynchronized steps, so two launches could both judge the same lock stale and the slower delete would destroy the lock the faster one had just created — leaving both processes sharing one `--user-data-dir`, the precise failure the broker exists to prevent, in the post-crash state where stale locks exist. A per-slot `.reclaim` mutex now serializes the re-check, delete, and re-create; losers fail fast to the next slot, and a mutex abandoned mid-reclaim ages out through the existing staleness threshold. Inherited lease cleanup also releases by token alone: it runs in the NW.js window, whose `process.pid` is never the spawned process pid the parent recorded, so the pid comparison could not match and the handler never fired.
- New map rollback: `saveMapProperties` inserted the MapInfos entry and renumbered its siblings before writing anything, then returned `false` on a write failure without undoing either. A failed map or MapInfos write now restores the previous list wholesale, since insertion mutates sibling `order` values that removing the new entry alone would not repair.
- Synchronous per-draw GPU texture upload under PIXI 8: `Bitmap` ends every draw op — `drawText`, `blt`, the fills — with `this._baseTexture.update()`. On v5 that was a dirty flag and the upload happened once at bind time during render; on v8 `GlTextureSystem` subscribes to the source's `update` event and `onSourceUpdate` performs a full synchronous `texImage2D` of the entire canvas. A `Window_VictoryExp` gauge count-up therefore issued 246 `drawText` plus 80 `blt` uploads in a single frame, measured at 106ms of a 128ms frame, while the canvas 2D work behind them totalled 0.4ms — both call sites landed at ~0.32ms each despite doing unrelated work, because the cost was entirely the shared upload tail. `pixi_compat` now queues sources instead and drains the queue from `AbstractRenderer.prototype.render`. Flushing per render rather than per frame is deliberate: render-to-texture passes that run mid-update, such as MVNovaLighting's light map, upload first as well, so no draw can read a stale texture. The queue is swapped before draining so an update raised during the flush defers to the next one, destroyed sources are skipped, and the behavior is gated on v8 — off v8 `update()` stays immediate, since no flush hook is installed there. Coverage in `pixi-texture-upload-batching.test.cjs`.
- ReactorProfiler battle-side attribution: the `windows` phase wraps only `Window_Base.prototype.update`, so a subclass that redraws its contents from its own update — `Window_VictoryExp.updateTicks` calling `drawAllGauges` every frame — contributed nothing to it and landed in the unattributed remainder of `update`. A battle capture could therefore report 108ms of a 110ms frame inside the tick handler with 0.4ms of `windows` and no way to place the rest. Added phases for `Scene_Battle.update`, `Spriteset_Battle.update`, `Window_Base.drawTextEx`, and `Bitmap.drawText`/`getPixel`/`blt`/`gradientFillRect`, so content drawing and the battle spriteset are attributed rather than inferred. `getPixel` is broken out on its own because every text-colour lookup passes through it.
- `ColorManager.textColor` windowskin readback per call: every colour lookup ran `Bitmap.getPixel`, which allocates an `ImageData` for a single pixel and builds its result string from three `toString(16)` conversions. Windows that redraw per frame multiply that quickly — `Window_VictoryExp.drawAllGauges` issues roughly ten lookups per actor per frame, so a six-member party sustained about 3,600 one-pixel reads per second purely to re-derive a fixed palette. The palette is immutable once the skin has loaded, so lookups are now cached per windowskin instance, keyed on the bitmap itself: swapping or reloading a skin replaces the instance and drops the cache, `loadWindowskin` clears it explicitly, and an unready bitmap bypasses the cache so a placeholder colour never becomes permanent. `readTextColor` retains the original uncached behavior. Coverage in `color-manager-cache.test.cjs`.
- MZ actor animation auto-mirroring applied to MV games: `Spriteset_Base.createAnimationSprite` runs `if (this.animationShouldMirror(targets[0])) mirror = !mirror`, and MZ's `animationShouldMirror` is `target.isActor()` — so every animation played on a party member is flipped horizontally. MV's `rpg_sprites.js` defines no such function, so MV content is authored for unmirrored playback and each cell's own `cell[5]` flag is the only intended flip. The reversal affected all actor-targeted animations but was only legible on ones containing text: Star Shift Rebellion's Counter animation (id 370, `img/animations/counter.png`, `mirror` passed as `false`, every cell's `cell[5]` zero) rendered its lettering backwards. `installAnimationMirrorCompatibility` overrides the rule to return false, in Tier 2 so MZ-authored projects are untouched. Coverage in `mv-animation-mirror.test.cjs`, which pins the stock MZ rule as the baseline before asserting the override.
- Null message sub-windows on unassociated `Window_Message` instances: `installMessageSubWindowsCompatibility` guarded only the MV field names (`_choiceWindow`, `_numberWindow`, `_itemWindow`), but MZ's own message flow reads the MZ names directly — `startInput()` calls `this._choiceListWindow.start()` with no null check. Only the scene's message window receives these through `associateWindows`, while plugins construct extra instances for measurement and backlog and custom battle scenes never associate at all; those instances still run the normal update loop, so the first pending choice threw `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'start')` and ended the session. The MZ names now resolve through the same fallback chain as the MV aliases — own window, then the scene's window, then the shared inert stand-in — so the choice starts on a real window and stays answerable rather than merely not crashing. The stand-in gained `start`, which MZ calls on the choice, number, and item windows unguarded. Regression coverage in `mv-message-subwindows.test.cjs` drives the real installer against stub windows.
- Stale MV interpreter character mirror: `installInterpreterCompatibility` mirrors the wait target onto `this._character` for commands 205/212/213 and lets `updateWaitMode` prefer it, but nothing ever cleared it. The map interpreter is a single reused instance whose `setup()` calls `clear()`, and MZ's `clear()` resets `_characterId` while knowing nothing about the mirror, so the previous event's character answered the next event's wait check. Having long since stopped moving, it reported "not waiting" on the first tick and dissolved any wait armed through the MZ contract alone — the same dissolving-wait failure the mirror was added to prevent, arriving from the opposite direction. The mirror is now dropped both in `clear()` and as each wait completes, by assignment rather than redefinition so the property stays non-enumerable and interpreters remain serializable. Regression coverage runs the real installer against a stub interpreter in `mv-interpreter-compat.test.cjs`.
- Bundled Demo runtime drift: `template/Demo/js` had fallen behind `runtime/` — 115 lines in `reactor_core.js`, 96 in `reactor_mv_compat.js`, 46 in `reactor_objects.js`, 15 in `libs/pixi_compat.js`, plus `reactor_windows.js` and `reactor_main.js` — and lacked `reactor_picture_extensions.js` and `libs/lz-string.js` altogether. Because `validateProjectRuntime` throws on any missing required file, Deploy Game rejected the Demo outright; `installRuntime` is a manual menu action and project creation overlays `runtime/` only for newly created projects, so nothing refreshed the Demo opened in place. All three templates are resynced with their own `reactor_plugins.js` preserved, and two tests now assert the tracked Demo is byte-identical to `runtime/` and satisfies both the deployment required-file list and its own `reactor_main.js` loader manifest.

## [0.95.0] - 2026-07-20

Cycle overview: [RPG Reactor 0.95.0: A More Complete Editor](../docs/devlogs/2026-07-18-rpg-reactor-0.95.0.md).

0.94.9 was an internal development version and was never published; its changes ship in 0.95.0.

### Bundled content

- Psychronic and Looseleaf are bundled Character Generator styles, with Psychronic as the default. Project JavaScript and PNG styles remain supported and load automatically.
- Portal is available in the Animation Generator and as a baked Effekseer Energy recipe. The release registries contain exactly 76 Animation Generator recipes and 106 Effekseer recipes across nine categories, including 15 Energy recipes.

### Added

- Localization: `I18nDeepTranslations.js` supplies a generated 2,411-phrase catalog for every non-English locale and loads before `I18nManager`. Static source inventory detects `tText` calls plus consumed array/object schemas, while tests enforce identical locale key sets, placeholder preservation, translated Terms labels, and Arabic RTL direction. Broad editor routing now covers database and event-command editors, Forge tools, map/project controls, deployment UI, and the browser host while leaving project-authored data untouched.
- Control Variables Game Data: the operand row now opens a nested transactional selector modeled after RPG Maker. Item/Weapon/Armor/Actor choices use padded database IDs and names, Actor/Enemy/Character properties use stock parameter lists, Character choices include current-map events, and Party/Other/Last Action Data expose their canonical operands. The parent row displays a readable summary while preserving the exact stock `[type, param1, param2]` tuple. Last Action Data and Troop Lower/Upper Layer labels have explicit translations and no-English-fallback coverage in all 17 non-English locales.
- Release engineering: `release-editor.cjs` packages the tracked Reactor One Demo starter, rejects dirty publish sources, disables unverified bundled-runtime reuse, and validates pinned NW.js archives. Per-target manifests bind version, target, signing state, source commit, size, and SHA-256. GitHub publication accepts only a complete matching Linux/Windows/macOS/Web candidate set and uploads those exact bytes without rebuilding; Windows uses verified Authenticode metadata/signing, macOS uses hardened-runtime signing/notarization/stapling, and optional itch publication uses a pinned hash-verified Butler binary.
- Security and lifecycle: privileged HTML surfaces escape project-authored names, notes, messages, traits, JSON, and Forge values, and Character Generator folder opening uses argument-array execution. Project creation rejects traversal/reserved/non-empty destinations, same-project locks use exclusive no-follow token ownership, stale map loads cannot replace current state, and critical writes use exclusive random temporary files plus file/directory flushing.
- Database workspace: the modal now occupies nearly the full viewport with fixed shell chrome and independently scrolling navigation, list, and detail panes. Web layouts retain the same scroll ownership and reflow the specialized Types and Terms workspaces at narrow breakpoints.
- Cross-instance authored-data clipboard: `ReactorClipboard` carries versioned typed JSON over the NW.js/system clipboard with shared-file and localStorage fallbacks. Top-level database lists publish backward-compatible versioned `databaseEntry` payloads containing one or more ID-free records; Ctrl/Cmd- and Shift-selection, Ctrl/Cmd+A, batch Cut/Copy/Paste, and the context menu work across Actors, Classes, Skills, Items, Weapons, Armors, Enemies, Troops, States, Animations, Common Events, and Tilesets. Batch paste overwrites consecutive slots while retaining destination IDs, one Undo snapshot covers the batch, and lazy-list refresh restores selection, rendered depth, and scroll position. Whole maps retain their existing map/MapInfos/tileset transport. Individual trait/effect rows publish dedicated typed payloads; project-defined element, state, skill, Skill/Weapon/Armor/Equipment Type, and Common Event IDs resolve by a unique exact name in the target project, while missing or duplicate references reject the paste. Sentinel, enum, parameter, and flag IDs remain unchanged. Map, Common, and Troop event command lists support structural multi-selection and external-first typed payloads; block expansion protects branches/continuations, destination indentation is rebased, and clipboard publication completes before Cut deletes. Async operations abort if the project, category, focus, selection, destination data, row owner, event, page, troop, or route changes while clipboard access is pending. Incompatible categories/types are rejected; other referenced assets and numeric references are copied as authored.
- Types: five simultaneous dense panels preserve reserved ID 0 and every later numeric reference. Toggle/range selection, arrows, Enter/F2, Ctrl/Cmd+A/X/C/V, Delete/Backspace, toolbar actions, and a custom context menu support multi-row editing. Clipboard text preserves trailing blank rows, paste can safely grow the target array, async generation guards prevent stale clipboard completion, interior clear never renumbers IDs, and destructive maximum reduction requires confirmation.
- Terms: Basic Statuses and Parameters use paired compact grids, Commands uses a four-column grid, and all keyed Messages occupy a grouped Type/Text table with independent scrolling. Every text field has Cut/Copy/Paste/Select All context actions with delayed-clipboard guards.
- Conditional Branch: complete MZ code-111 support for condition types 0-13 and their subtypes, including variable RHS, Actor predicates, Enemy state/appeared checks, Character direction, equipment inclusion flags, Pressed/Triggered/Repeated buttons, Script, and all vehicles. No-change edits preserve original parameter arrays exactly; new configurations emit canonical arrays and command summaries cover every form.
- Advanced event commands: Control Variables adds a structured Advanced Expression operand covering arithmetic, power, min/max, `atan2`, random, bitwise operations, absolute value, square root, and degree-based sine/cosine. Loop uses direct stock Loop/Repeat Above insertion while preserving existing nested bodies; previously generated finite-loop structures remain byte-identical if encountered. Common Event keeps direct calls as stock code 117 and adds variable Common Event/current-map page calls. Conditional Branch adds extended keyboard pressed/triggered/repeated/released states, independent left/middle/right mouse states, wheel directions, and pointer X/Y comparisons. The same editors and indent-aware insertion behavior are available in Map, Common Event, and Troop command lists.
- Portable generated-command codec: `ReactorEventCommandCodec` emits strict canonical versioned tags only inside stock Script-bearing command layouts (111, 122, and 355). Generated JavaScript is syntax-checked before authoring, exact tag/body agreement is required when reopening, and malformed, edited, or unknown-version tags remain ordinary Script commands. Generated dynamic picture commands include direct stock MV/MZ fallback bodies rather than requiring Reactor to interpret custom parameter fields.
- Picture command extensions: Show/Move/Erase support variable picture IDs; Move supports variable duration; Erase supports One, Range, and All with direct or variable endpoints. Show and Move add initial/fixed/tweened angle, custom anchors, sine-wave offsets, and Overlay blend state. Negative scale uses `-2000..2000` and Quick Setting previews it directly instead of adding a separate flip control. The isolated `reactor_picture_extensions.js` module validates old-save defaults and runtime payloads; Overlay uses the advanced blend only when back-buffer support is active and otherwise degrades to Normal without warnings.
- Picture Quick Setting and movement preview: code 231's Quick Setting button now shares the Image row and opens a responsive surface using `System.advanced.screenWidth/screenHeight`, with MZ defaults for older projects. The screen grid occupies the center of a workspace extending one full screen beyond every edge; pointer capture continues updating X/Y beyond the visible workspace up to the command's ±9,999 range, so off-screen entrances are authorable instead of clamping at the screen edge. The fixed 1,000px dialog ceiling is gone: each layout pass measures wrapped controls and shell chrome, fits the exact project-aspect workspace against both available viewport dimensions, and recalculates on normal window and fullscreen resize. Origin, X/Y, independent width/height scale, and opacity are editable in the modal and remain transactional until Apply. Code 232 exposes the same controls plus live Duration frame entry and replayable easing animation: changing Duration affects the next Preview immediately, while Apply synchronizes it back to the main command. Event and common-event callers provide the current command list/index, the editor scans preceding Show/Move/Erase commands for that picture ID, preserves the latest image and endpoint, and animates from it using RPG Maker's four easing curves. No preceding visible Show produces an intentionally empty preview.
- Map-region sampling: `MapEditor` owns a dedicated six-plane stamp instead of forcing sampled map data through palette-layer/autotile placement rules. Right-drag captures a normalized, map-clipped rectangle from z0-z3, shadow z4, and region z5; the immutable stamp preserves exact autotile shapes and zero values. A side-effect-free canvas composite renders normal tiles, autotiles, higher tiles, shadows, and visible region tint into the hover preview, with an outlined footprint fallback for oversized selections. Pencil left-click/drag writes all planes directly, crops destination overflow, sends changed z0-z4 cells through one `updateTiles` batch, refreshes changed regions separately, and uses the existing begin/commit snapshot so a stroke creates one Undo entry while sampling creates none. Palette/region/tool/map/Event-mode changes clear the stamp. Event interaction teardown now unregisters only its named handlers instead of stripping map painting, sampling, and middle/Shift panning listeners from their shared PIXI container.
- Shift autotile placement: holding Shift while painting A1-A4 with Pencil, Rectangle, or Circle stores the selected shape directly without reconnecting painted tiles or reshaping their neighbors. Other layers and tools retain Shift-drag panning.
- A1 animation preference: `rr-settings.animateAutotiles` defaults on and is exposed as a checkbox in File → Options plus a compact synchronized `A1` checkbox beside the map zoom and coordinates. `TilemapManager.setA1AnimationEnabled` adds/removes the PIXI ticker without rebuilding the map; disabling resets both water sequences to frame zero and refreshes tracked A1 textures. The preference is global, survives sessions and project switches, and affects only the editor preview.
- Tests: added focused cross-instance clipboard, MV save, MV plugin visual, System 2 Magic Skills, event-branch structure, Event-mode double-click, database workspace, preview cleanup, localization source-audit, Unicode asset/index, project-open retry, package preflight, and PIXI pre-init game-loop coverage.
- Recursive assets: new shared `AssetFiles.js` performs deterministic recursive discovery without following symlinks, deduplicates extensionless names by declared format priority, normalizes stored separators to `/`, and resolves encoded desktop/Web preview URLs. It now backs BGM/BGS/ME/SE players and selectors, animation sheets/effects/timing SE, character/face/battler/tileset/battleback/parallax/title pickers, movement-route SE, and plugin `@type file` parameters. Core RPG Maker pickers expose only lowercase `.ogg`/`.png` files that the runtime can reconstruct; `$`/`!$` classification reads the final filename inside a nested path across every character preview surface.
- Picker navigation: new shared `PickerIndex.js` provides Audio-compatible Unicode grapheme sections, deterministic locale/numeric ordering, accent-insensitive Unicode case folding, and full-relative-path search. Its reusable browser adds a search field, clickable section rail, sticky headers, active-section tracking, and keyboard-selectable listbox options to `DatabaseEditorUI.showImagePicker`, `CharacterGraphicPicker`, and the Show Text face picker. Reopening an event character restores its exact file/index/pattern/direction, while a generation guard prevents slow stale image loads from replacing a newer selection.
- Picker search styling: search fields use a high-contrast theme-aware deep fill, accent border, and visible focus ring. A Reactor-styled accent clear button replaces Chromium's native blue search-cancel icon.
- Picker section navigation: clicking an earlier Unicode section after jumping downward now scrolls the picker list back up correctly. Navigation calculates the header's normal-flow position from preceding row heights and assigns the list's `scrollTop` directly, avoiding both `scrollIntoView` and Chromium's sticky-header offset reporting.
- System 1 start locations: Player, Boat, Ship, and Airship rows retain manual Map/X/Y inputs and add a visual Browse action backed by the Transfer Player map tree/canvas. Selection state is local until OK, each triplet commits together, nested expansion state survives tree rebuilds, search reaches nested maps, overlapping async renders cannot replace the latest preview, and the live `TilemapManager` is supplied through the database project proxy. The Title Screen picker now uses `RRPickerIndex.createBrowser` for searchable Unicode sections beside its image preview.
- Plugin search and actions: the load-order pane filters loaded plugins live by name, description, or author without losing their source indexes, while Add Plugin filters unused plugin filenames. Remove Plugin and Save Changes occupy a persistent shell-level footer in that order, so Save remains available with empty, single, or multiple selection. Source-derived `@help` documentation has separate literal case-insensitive highlighting, active/total match status, clear and wraparound previous/next controls, Enter/Shift+Enter and F3/Shift+F3 navigation, and a Plugin Manager-scoped Ctrl/Cmd+F shortcut. Highlight rendering uses text nodes rather than HTML, reloads clear stale help state, nested parameter/add dialogs suspend manager shortcuts, and controls expose accessible labels/live status without changing plugin manifests.
- Complex Plugin Parameters: struct parsing now terminates at each actual `*/`, so later schemas such as `YEP_OptionsCore`'s nested `Options` definition are available instead of producing “Could not find struct definition.” Schema-guided one-layer decoding preserves JSON-looking note/string leaves and simple-array strings; nested editors receive the source plugin's complete definition set, clone drafts for Cancel, support struct and scalar arrays, and synchronize Text edits before returning to Structure. Named rows replace JSON spew and add Edit/Delete/up/down controls, double-click editing, and full-row drag-and-drop ordering with theme-accent insertion feedback. Alternating list surfaces, stronger table framing, and neutral group headers derive entirely from active theme tokens; element editors use OK rather than an ambiguous Close action. New entries use schema defaults, notes use multiline editors, and stable grids align labels and fields. Saving recursively restores RPG Maker's array-of-JSON-strings format. A real nested `YEP_OptionsCore` fixture decodes and re-encodes byte-for-byte.

### Changed

- Bumped current development version to RPG Reactor 0.95.0.
- System 2 `magicSkills` now renders as ordered Skill Type dropdown rows with a trailing fill row. Choosing None removes a row, duplicates are removed, missing IDs remain visible until edited, values stay numeric, and simply opening System 2 does not initialize a missing field.
- Troops and Animations now use dense, viewport-bounded database layouts. Troops uses a two-column upper workspace: the Battle Preview fills the left side while Battle Test, Members, Battleback, and Note stack in a 240-300px right sidebar; narrow web layouts collapse to one column, and Battle Events stays full-width below. Its preview reproduces runtime box centering, MZ/MV vertical offsets, bottom-anchored enemy sprites, side/front battler priority, and battleback sizing from Screen/UI Area dimensions; drag coordinates invert that transform before saving. Battle Test equipment filters by actor-plus-class weapon/armor permissions and sealed slots, completely omits slots with no valid/current item, retains actionable stale items visibly without counting them in stats, and leaves room between dropdowns and the scrollbar. Battle Test Configuration and Conditions use distinct theme-aware headers, scrollable bodies, and separated footers; Conditions also uses a stable label/control grid with alternating rows. Animations removes the fixed 470px/600px preview requirements, places its two sprite properties side by side, shortens the sheet strip, narrows frame/effect controls, and keeps long effect/preview controls locally scrollable. Animation canvas backing sizes stay unchanged, and CSS-to-backing coordinate conversion keeps sprite drops, hit tests, and dragging accurate at every visual scale.
- Troop Members replaces its add dropdown and one-off replacement browser with one `RRPickerIndex` modal. Enemy names lead the Unicode section/search labels, database IDs remain visible/searchable, and the right pane previews the resolved front/side-view battler with stats. Add opens the picker directly; a selected row replaces through double-click, Enter, the swap button, or its context menu. The focusable list and battle canvas share local Ctrl/Cmd+C/X/V and Delete handling, paste inserts an offset deep clone, and the context menu mirrors Add/Replace/Cut/Copy/Paste/Delete without leaking Delete to the database sidebar.

### Fixed

- Interactive editor deployment now uses developer-build runtime policy instead of immutable release policy. Latest Stable and user-selected NW.js versions may download with a clear unauthenticated-development warning, while release CLI builds still require pinned hashes. Cross-platform Windows packages skip ESM-only `resedit` metadata editing inside NW.js workers, fixing Linux-hosted Windows SDK builds; release candidates still apply and verify app-owned metadata under standalone Node before signing.
